# 📜 THE REGISTER OF LOCKS — every ruling the founder has made, one page

> **READ THIS FIRST, EVERY SESSION** (CLAUDE.md § Session start). This is the founder's memory, externalised. It exists because on **6 Aug** he said: *"i cant remember everything fable. and i am struggling here. because things slip far to often."*
>
> **What this is.** Every decision the founder has locked, with **his own words where they exist**, the source it came from, and — stated honestly — what actually enforces it. Not strategy, not a roadmap, not status: those have their own homes. A rule nobody enforces is a wish, and this page says which is which.
>
> ## ⛓️ THE CHAIN RULE — the one that earned this rewrite
> **A superseded decision is NEVER deleted. It is chained: `old → amended`, both dated, and the LATEST WINS.**
>
> On 6 Aug I contradicted the founder's own #577 amendment within hours of him relying on it — and **the cause was sitting on this very page**: rule **D1** still read *"Instantly sends for US… by API"*, the **26-Jul** lock, with no sign that the founder had amended it on **30 Jul**. I read the stale half, paraphrased it, and it merged. Every automated check stayed green, because doc-lint verifies counts and copies, **never whether a sentence is true**. The only check that fired was the founder's memory — which is precisely the thing this page is meant to replace.
>
> **So: an un-chained supersession on this page is a live trap.** If you amend a rule, the old text stays, struck and dated, directly above the new one.
>
> ## The rules about these rules
> 1. **Cite by date.** No sentence about a locked decision may be written anywhere — doc, PR body or chat — without re-reading the lock here and citing its date (CLAUDE.md, the citation law).
> 2. **This is a REGISTER, not a new home for truth.** Every row cites its source. **Status lives only in PRODUCT-INVENTORY.** If a row here and its source disagree, that is a bug — fix it at the source, the same session.
> 3. **Same-session capture.** A ruling the founder makes in chat becomes a row here **that session** (CLAUDE.md ritual 4b). A ruling that lives only in a transcript is a ruling that will be contradicted — transcripts are not read at session start and cannot be grepped.
> 4. **Verbatim beats paraphrase.** Where the founder's words exist, they are quoted. My summary of his words is not his ruling.

## 2 · SAFETY — the rules that protect a real person

| # | Rule | Where it came from | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **A demo account can NEVER touch a real prospect.** `is_demo` is a hard stop inside the send path, and every demo address is `.invalid`. | founder-locked | `figsy.ts:543` |
| S2 | **The kill-switch is OFF until the founder says otherwise.** Nothing reaches a real prospect while it is off — including consent sends. | #344, the five consent doors | `AUTO_OUTREACH_ENABLED !== 'true'` |
| S3 | **Minimum 20 approvals before work starts.** A lifetime commitment, not per batch. | #538 | `MIN_BATCH_APPROVALS = 20`, resolved against the DB |
| S4 | **A paying client with no approvals for 30 days is suspended** (warned at 23). | #538 | `COLD_DAYS = 30`, `WARN_DAYS = 23` |
| S5 | **Never cold-email from the primary domain.** ⛓️ **ENFORCED 20 Aug (HC-4) — this column read `—` for 25 days.** The consent email — a cold send to a stranger — went out from `hello@get-kind.com`, the domain every invoice and password reset also leaves from, so a spam complaint from someone who never asked to hear from us landed on the reputation of our billing mail. It now sends via `COLD_FROM`, and `FIGSY_COLD_FROM` is **boot-critical**: `deliverability.ts` falls back to the transactional identity when it is unset, so at `important` the rule was one forgotten variable away from silently reversing. ⛓️ Supersedes the **30 Jul #547 decision** (*"consent stays on hello@get-kind.com for now"*), whose two premises had both become false — the variable is set (`K.I.N.D <figsy@gettingkind.com>`, founder screenshot 20 Aug) and `gettingkind.com` is a verified, DKIM/SPF/DMARC-configured cold domain. That entry's own closing line asked for it to be revisited before the first-send ladder. | founder, 26 Jul · enforced 20 Aug | `lib/email.ts` (`from: COLD_FROM`) · `startup-check.ts` (`FIGSY_COLD_FROM` critical) · `consent-email-hc4.test.ts` |
| S6 | **Opt-outs are global**, checked at sourcing, across every client. ⛓️ **PARTLY ENFORCED 20 Aug (HC-4) — and this column read `—` because one path genuinely did not check.** The consent email asked **six callers'** worth of strangers for permission and **not one of them consulted the blocklist**: a person who opted out of client A's outreach, freshly sourced for client B and scored over 60, was emailed by name and asked for consent they had already refused. The probe now lives inside `sendConsentEmail` — where all six doors funnel — normalised per HC-1 and **fail-closed**, since that function IS the send and has no gate behind it. ⚠️ **Still not a complete claim:** this fixes the consent path; whether the SCORING and enrichment pipeline consults the blocklist at all is the open **F2** question (GDPR Art. 21 objects to *processing*, not only to contact), and until that is answered *"checked at sourcing"* is aspirational for part of the pipeline. | verified 26 Jul · consent path 20 Aug | `lib/email.ts` (blocklist + `isSuppressed` inside `sendConsentEmail`) · **F2 still open** |

### 5a · THE CLAUSE TABLE — the rule that exists because I kept getting this wrong

> **Why this is a required artifact and not a promise.** A behaviour I undertake, I forget: I committed to a clause-by-clause read after Prompt 1 and had dropped it by Prompt 4, where I reported an integration complete while **nothing called it**. An artifact is different — if it is missing from the PR, the founder sends it back without reading a line of code, exactly as the pasted-red rule already works.
>
> **Every miss this week was invisible to grep**: a module nothing imported, a generator writing to a scratchpad, a green test *requiring* the broken value, a refusal rendered in the success colour. Grep only ever returns what you already suspected.

**Paste this BEFORE the work prompt. It governs the message that follows it.**

```
BEFORE YOU BUILD ANYTHING. This governs the prompt in my NEXT message, and
every report you make about it.

1. READ, DON'T GREP. Before you touch a path, read that path end to end —
   the whole file, not the function you intend to edit. Grep can tell you a
   thing exists; it can never tell you a thing is missing. If you use grep,
   say so and say what it could not have shown you.

2. BUILD THE CLAUSE TABLE FIRST. Break my next prompt into every separate
   clause, QUOTED FROM MY WORDS — not paraphrased. Paraphrasing is how "every
   /operator endpoint" became "the System screen". Show me that table before
   you start building, so I can correct the reading before you write code.

3. REPORT AGAINST THAT TABLE. Your final report and your PR must open with:

   | Clause (my words) | Built | Verified how |

   "Verified how" must name the command you ran or the file you read end to
   end. "I built it" is not verification. "grepped for callers — nothing
   calls it" is.

4. ANY ❌ GOES IN THE FIRST LINE of the report and the PR title. Not the
   bottom. If a clause is partly built, it is ❌ with the gap named — there
   is no ✅ with an asterisk.

5. NEVER SAY A PROMPT IS DONE WITHOUT THAT TABLE. If you catch yourself
   about to, stop and build the table instead.

6. IF A CLAUSE CANNOT BE BUILT, say NOT-POSSIBLE and why. Do not work around
   it silently and do not substitute something easier.

Confirm you have read this, then wait for my next message.
```

**The two clauses that were earned, not designed:**

- **Clause 2 (table BEFORE the work)** would have caught Prompt 4. *"on approved leads, the product creates the campaign in Instantly, pushes the lead"* would have sat there as a row from the start, and its missing ✅ would have been visible at the end instead of surviving into a PR that read as complete.
- **Clause 4 (❌ at the top)** exists because the NOT-POSSIBLE items went in a tidy section near the bottom while the report led with what worked. The founder had to ask.

### 7b · THE 6 AUGUST RULINGS, PART TWO — four open questions closed in one message

> **The founder, 6 Aug:** *"all with your recomendfaion. lets close these off."* Four questions that had each been open for days or weeks. His word closed all four; the reasoning below each is mine, recorded so it can be argued with later.

| # | Ruling | What it amends |
|---|---|---|
| **A10** | **THE SEQUENCE CAP IS 7.** The 8-Jul lock said 10 (*"no it is 10. we know this"*). The client's own action is now **`POST /icps/:id/proof`**: up to 20 real masked leads, `is_active` stays FALSE, no campaign, no reveal, no charge, no enrolment, no send. The client asks to SEE; K.I.N.D still decides when it RUNS. ⚠️ **AMENDED AGAIN 22 Aug (integration): ONE CORE ICP, AND LEARNING IS NOT GO.** Two pieces that were each correct alone collided. ① Every client save **INSERTED** an ICP, so the refinement between pass 1 and pass 2 produced a **second experiment** rather than a sharper version of the same one — pass 1's leads and feedback orphaned on a row nobody read again. The client's FIRST ICP is still created; every later save **UPDATES it, preserving `icp.id`**, and never writes `is_active`. Both halves of this rule then hold together: a paying client's revision reaches their LIVE targeting with no gate (25 Jul) and nobody outside K.I.N.D activates anything (22 Aug). ⚠️ **AMENDED ONCE MORE 22 Aug — A LIVE CLIENT'S CHANGE WAITS FOR US.** Founder-ruled verbatim: *"If an ALREADY-LIVE client changes/refines their targeting: **THE CHANGE MUST WAIT FOR K.I.N.D. REVIEW.** It must NOT take effect immediately. Milla may save the revised targeting and understanding, but K.I.N.D. must review it and press GO again before the revised targeting becomes operational."* ⚠️ **This REVERSES my earlier reading of the 25-Jul half.** *"No gate on their own change"* still means a live client may revise freely and we never hold their edit hostage — but the edit is now **saved and held**, not applied. The row could not tell CURRENT LIVE targeting from a REVISED PENDING one (the targeting columns ARE what `runIcpJob` sources from), so two additive columns are that distinction: `icps.pending_targeting` + `icps.pending_submitted_at`. Nothing reads them for sourcing, scoring or sending; Vida badges the ICP card *"revision waiting since …, not in effect"*; **GO reviews-and-applies** in the same write that activates, and clears them so a second GO cannot re-apply a stale revision. A live client's **campaign brief waits with it, and is not lost** — founder-ruled the same day, closing the gap left by the first pass: *"For an ALREADY-LIVE client, BOTH: 1. revised targeting 2. revised campaign intent / campaign outcome / brief must WAIT for K.I.N.D review and GO. Neither becomes operational immediately."* The first pass refused to APPLY the new brief and then **dropped** it — right not to use it, wrong to lose it. It is now held on `icps.pending_campaign_intent` beside the pending targeting, so a refused GO cannot clear half a revision, and GO applies **both**. ⚠️ It needs its own column rather than a key inside `pending_targeting`, and that is provable rather than a preference: GO applies the held revision by spreading that payload straight onto the `icps` row, so every key in it becomes an `icps` column — and `campaign_intent` is a `figsy_campaigns` column (`20260524_campaign_intent.sql`). ⚠️ **AND "TOGETHER" IS NOW LITERALLY TRUE (22 Aug, atomicity correction).** The first implementation applied the revision with **two ordinary client writes** — the campaign brief, then the ICP — which is not a transaction: the first could land and the second fail, leaving a client with a **NEW BRIEF and OLD TARGETING**, FIGSY writing for an audience nobody approved, while the revision still looked like it was waiting. Ordering them more carefully cannot fix that and a compensating rollback is just a third thing that can fail. GO now calls one `SECURITY DEFINER` function, **`apply_pending_revision`** — a plpgsql body IS one transaction, so the brief, the targeting, the one-active sweep, the activation and the clearing of all three pending fields land **together**, and any raise inside rolls back **every** part. Failures decided *before* any write (bad args, an ICP that is not this client's) return a clean refusal instead, which is what keeps the function probeable. The targeting is applied **field by field from a nine-column whitelist**, so a stray key in the stored payload can never reach a column nobody intended. His reason for the whole shape: *"The founder wants to learn how clients behave before making this more efficient. For launch, correctness and control matter more than automation."* **An unpaid prospect is untouched:** nothing of theirs is running, so their refinement still edits in place and pass 2 needs no review step. ② `persistMillaUnderstanding` parked `campaign_intent` by calling `ensureCampaignForIcp`, which **created ACTIVE** — so *telling Milla what you want made a campaign live*, before payment, before an operator looked, and that live campaign then **refused the operator's own GO** through the one-active invariant. Milla now scaffolds a **draft** campaign (`figsy.ts` selects `status = 'active'`, so a draft's intent is stored and invisible to generation until GO), and `{ activate: true }` — passed only by K.I.N.D's GO and by an operator creating an ICP in Vida — is the sole path that makes a campaign live. The confirmation from *"this represents us"* is recorded on `clients.milla_understanding_confirmed_at` — auditable, and read by nothing before activation, generation or sending, exactly as this rule requires |

| **AR17** | 🎁 **FREE REAL-LEAD PROOF — THE LAUNCH ACQUISITION MOTION, AND ITS FENCE.** A prospect is shown **REAL masked leads before they pay**: pass 1 up to 20, one refinement of the SAME core ICP, pass 2 up to 20 more, then **no third automatic batch** — a human conversation takes over. Proof proves **TARGETING FIT, not deliverability**: contact details stay masked, `revealed_at` stays NULL, and there is **no Hunter, no Apollo reveal, no $4, no wallet, no pack slot, no enrolment and no sending** before payment. Feedback is *looks right* / *not a fit* — never the commercial word **Approve**. ⚠️ **THE SPEND FENCE, founder-set 22 Aug: 40 PDL records per prospect across BOTH passes = $11.20 at the verified $0.28 rate**, plus a **$300/month free-acquisition ceiling**. That $300 is *"the maximum acquisition PDL spend I am initially prepared to fund"* — most prospects are expected to convert before consuming a full two-pass allowance; when volume becomes meaningful the founder steps in as the salesperson; and if the company is earning and demand justifies it he **deliberately raises the ceiling**. It is a funding decision, never a business ceiling, and it is **never raised automatically**. ⚠️ **POOL FIRST IS MANDATORY AND FREE** — owned records cost $0 and do **not** consume the 40. **This is NOT total CAC**: it fences bought data only, not the Claude calls behind Milla, the ICP builder or scoring, which the repo prices nowhere. ⚠️ **AMENDED 22 Aug (same day), founder-ruled on review:** *"Do NOT interpret the 40-record PDL lifetime fence as the pass-size rule."* They are **two separate rules and conflating them is wrong in both directions** — **20 = leads SURFACED per automatic pass** (customer experience), **40 = PDL records bought across both passes** (spend). Pool records are free and never touch the 40, but they **DO fill the 20**: pool 13 → at most **7** PDL for that pass · pool 20 → **no PDL at all** · pool 25 → still only **20 surfaced**. The build had used the paid client's per-run target (200 for a $299 pack) as the proof pass size, so *"up to 20 real masked leads"* existed in the founder's model and nowhere in the code. ⚠️ **AMENDED AGAIN 22 Aug (round 4): PROOF IS AN ACTION, NEVER AN ACCOUNT PROPERTY.** The first build inferred a proof run from *"this account has never been funded"*, which meant a nightly cron, an operator kick, an admin route or a partner route touching a prospect would silently burn one of their two passes and reserve acquisition money nobody had decided to spend. The pass is now claimed by the client's own proof route and travels INTO the run, so a run can never claim one itself; a run without it is an ordinary run whoever the account is. A funded account reaching proof mode sources **nothing** — it never falls through to the paid path, because the two budgets are separate (AR18). | **40 records · $11.20 · $300/mo** (founder-set 22 Aug) | founder-ruled 22 Aug | `try_claim_proof_pass` · `try_reserve_proof_records` · `release_proof_records` (20260822_free_proof_acquisition) · `free-proof-fence.test.ts` + `free-proof-route.test.ts` |

Paid delivery keeps `pdl_monthly_cap_usd` and `sourcing_ledger`, **completely untouched**. ⚠️ **Why a second ledger rather than one shared sum:** if proof spend were written into `sourcing_ledger` it would count against the paid ceiling, and a busy acquisition month could refuse a PAYING client's sourcing. | **AR19** | 🛑 **CLIENT **PROOF** SOURCES THROUGH APOLLO — a bounded, founder-locked exception to AR5 (15 Sep, S2-RT-001A).** AR5's audience→provider table stands for every other flow: house → Apollo, client → PDL. **The FREE PROOF journey is the one exception**, and it is scoped to Proof, not to clients. ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED IT, and it is a live customer, not a hypothetical.** Northstar Revenue completed its Brief, promoted to a client with an active ICP, and entered Proof normally. AR5 routed the client audience to PDL, PDL answered **402 — search credits exhausted**, and the run resolved fail-closed with zero prospects: claim released `run_failed`, `proof_passes_done` stayed 0, and the customer was shown *"We hit a snag confirming your matches"*. **Nothing was broken.** Proof was pointed at a provider with nothing left to give, and six forensic rounds disproved a crash that never happened. ⚠️ **NO FALLBACK, EITHER WAY.** A Proof run Apollo cannot serve **FAILS CLOSED** — it does not quietly try PDL, because "try the other provider" is the silent cross-over AR5 exists to prevent, and a prospect served off an unintended stack is the defect wearing a success message. No Apollo∪PDL union, no double-sourcing. ⚠️ **AR8 IS UNAFFECTED** — Proof was never behind the PDL cash fence; it has its own (`try_reserve_proof_records`) and writes no `sourcing_ledger` row, so moving it to Apollo fabricates no PDL cost. ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE, STATED: new Proof leads are Apollo-backed**, so they carry `apollo_id` and are revealable through `apolloRevealableIds()`. That is a deliberate effect of this exception, not an AR15 drain. ⚠️ **AND THE TRUST RULE MOVED WITH IT:** search-trust promotion is keyed on the PROVIDER, not the audience — `audience === 'house'` named the Apollo branch only while Apollo and house were the same thing, and left a COMPLETED Apollo search that honestly matched nobody recorded as `failed`, i.e. the same snag state one branch further down. | *"CLIENT PROOF SOURCING MUST USE APOLLO."* · *"DO NOT use PDL for client Proof."* · *"DO NOT restore PDL credits as the solution."* · *"DO NOT add PDL as a fallback for Proof."* · *"Do not reinterpret this as authority to redesign every sourcing flow in the product."* | | founder-locked 15 Sep | `lib/provider-boundary.ts` (`sourcingProviderFor(audience, { proofMode })` — a SEPARATE function, so the ICP preview's AR14 selector is untouched) · `lib/apollo.ts` (the one sourcing call site) · `routes/icps.ts` (`sourcingProvider` derived once; search-trust + PDL budget alarm keyed on it) · `proof-apollo-provider.test.ts` (15 assertions, **RED 12/14** pre-fix, 2 teeth proved) · ⛓️ **AR5** · **AR8** · **AR14** · **AR15** · **R106** |
| **AR20** | 🛑 **THE PROOF QUALIFICATION-ENRICHMENT EXCEPTION — internal, narrow, and it buys TRUTH, not contact details (15 Sep, founder-locked, S2-RT-001A).** During **FREE PROOF ONLY**, M&V may call Apollo's existing internal enrichment/reveal (`bulkMatchEmails` → `people/bulk_match`) **solely where it is necessary to prove a required qualification fact — today, GEOGRAPHY — before a prospect may be accepted into the Proof set.** The shape is **SOURCE → ENRICH REQUIRED FACTS → QUALIFY → ACCEPT OR REJECT**, and nothing else. ⚠️ **AN UNKNOWN QUALIFICATION FACT IS NOT A PASS.** Proven inside the customer's stated criteria is eligible · proven outside is rejected · **still unknown after enrichment is REJECTED, never deferred and never assumed.** ⚠️ **WHY IT EXISTS, proved against a LIVE payload and not inferred.** A real `mixed_people/api_search` response (captured 15 Sep) returns **availability booleans only** — `has_country: true`, `has_city`, `has_state`, `has_email` — with **no country value anywhere**, on the person or the organization, and `last_name_obfuscated` in place of the surname. The ONLY source of a country is `bulk_match`. Without this exception AR19 + *"unknown ≠ pass"* would reject every Apollo candidate and return an empty Proof set for any ICP that names a geography — the documented 250 → 0 defect, inside Proof. ⚠️ **IT CREATES NO COMMERCIAL AUTHORITY, and that is STRUCTURAL, not promised.** Only `.country` is read; the email, email status and surname the same call returns are **discarded and never reach the row**. So: **no payment authority · no outreach authority · no enrolment · no Make Live · no Run · no send · no client wallet or pack charge · no approval count · no ledger row**, and `revealed_at` **stays NULL**. **CLIENT-FACING CONTACT DETAILS REMAIN MASKED** — `/leads/for-approval` omits name, email and phone whatever the row holds, so nothing a prospect can see changes because an internal qualification step ran. ⚠️ **FENCED THREE WAYS** so no other flow can reach it: `proofMode` only · Apollo only · and only when the customer actually named the criterion. A Proof ICP with no geography asks nothing about location, so nothing is enriched and nothing is spent. ⚠️ **TRUTH TO THE CLIENT OUTRANKS THE CREDIT.** The founder ruled the spend necessary rather than show a prospect a person whose country was never established: *"truth to client is more important than minimising Apollo credits."* Post-MVP1, commercial soundness per booked meeting will analyse real provider costs — that analysis does not gate this rule. ⚠️ **NO NEW MONEY MODEL** — acquisition cost stays Apollo's existing 0 at this boundary and reveal-time credits remain unmodelled exactly as they are for House today; nothing is labelled PDL and **the client is never charged**. ⚠️ **AND THE HOUSE DEFERRAL IS UNCHANGED.** `geoAnswerComesLater` is correct for House, whose leads are revealed downstream and judged by `finalVerdict`; Proof is never revealed downstream — delivery is capped by the prospect's zero credit balance — so “later” never arrives and the deferral is switched OFF for `proofMode` alone. | *"UNKNOWN GEOGRAPHY ≠ PASS."* · *"A Proof prospect must NOT be surfaced unless geography is proven to match the ICP."* · *"geography unknown → reject / do not surface"* · *"Do NOT weaken targeting to manufacture Proof results."* · *"M&V may use Apollo's existing reveal/enrichment capability solely to establish required qualification facts such as geography BEFORE a prospect can enter the Proof set."* · *"This is a narrow internal qualification exception."* · *"truth to client is more important than minimising Apollo credits"* · *"Do NOT treat provider success as geography proof."* | founder-locked 15 Sep | `routes/icps.ts` (`proofGeoByApolloId` resolved BEFORE the contact loop, so a refusal costs no insert · `provenCountry` · `geoAnswerComesLater … && !proofMode` · the proven country persisted to `leads.country` and `lead_pool.country`) · `lib/apollo.ts` (`bulkMatchEmails` — **unchanged**: it writes nothing, touches no wallet, pack, payment or delivery state, and returns a Map) · `proof-apollo-provider.test.ts` (group G — accepted/rejected/unknown/no-answer/no-geography/masked, fixture shaped from the live payload) · `proof-provider-off.test.ts` (the original geography invariant, assertion unchanged) · ⛓️ **AR19** · **AR17** · **AR18** · **AR5** · **R106** |

The predicate is the smallest set of durable facts that does: **① not funded** — `fundedVia(credit_transactions) === null`, the EXACT classifier `/icps/:id/proof` refuses on and the money surfaces use; **② `proof_passes_done = 0`** — the same counter `claim_proof_authority` reads as `v_done`, and the only fact that sees a pre-ledger client, since `proof_started_at` exists only from 20260826 and was **never backfilled**; **③ `proof_started_at IS NULL`** — ② alone misses the client whose FIRST run CRASHED, whose claim was RELEASED and whose counter is therefore still 0. ⚠️ **NO CALIBRATION CHECK, and that is not an omission** — escalation, the granted restart and the refinement journey are all reachable only AFTER a Proof set exists, so ② and ③ exclude every one of them structurally. ⚠️ **AN UNREADABLE FACT IS NOT A PASS.** The route tolerates an unreadable funding read because a human is pressing its button; here nothing downstream asks a person anything, so *"we could not prove it"* **refuses**. Fail-closed costs the client nothing — the review stays resolved and their own Proof door still works. ⚠️ **THE TRANSLATION IS NEVER AT RISK.** The continuation runs AFTER the write, the replay fence and the audit; no branch rolls anything back, and a Proof failure never turns a saved resolution into an error response. | **AR22** | 🛑 **A VETOED BRIEF COMPLETION IS RECOVERED AS PRODUCT STATE — NOT AS A SENTENCE FROM MILLA, AND NEVER WITH A SECOND MODEL CALL (16 Sep, founder-locked, S1-RT-010).** ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED IT, and it stranded a fresh production signup.** Daniel Brooks / Cedar Peak Advisory reached **10 of 11** Brief facts — `exclusions` was never asked and never given — and the model declared `complete` anyway with *"Great, that's everything I need — thanks Daniel!"*. A sentence written to CLOSE the conversation was delivered as the turn meant to CONTINUE it: no plan rendered, no *"Confirm my brief"* rendered, the targeting panel stayed on its placeholder, and the last thing the customer was told was that nothing more was needed. **There was no next action a new person could have found.** 🛑 **THE AUTHORITY SPLIT, founder-stated.** The **APPLICATION** owns *"required facts · missing facts · completion authority · persisted truth · whether a response may advance"*; **MILLA** owns *"natural conversational wording"* — and nothing else moves between them. ⚠️ **SO THE SENTENCE IS SUPPRESSED, NOT REPLACED AND NOT RE-ROLLED.** Nothing writes English on Milla's behalf (R-unchanged: *"THE SENTENCE IS MILLA'S OR THERE IS NONE"*), and **no second provider call repairs the turn** — re-rolling a turn already paid for is how a product pays three times for one answer. The response instead carries the **authoritative** missing fact (`type: 'outstanding'` · `remaining` · `total` · `next {id,label}`) and the portal renders it as a **notice outside the message list**, with no avatar and no attribution. ⚠️ **ONE RESOLUTION, SO THE RECOVERY CANNOT NAME A DIFFERENT FACT FROM THE ONE THAT BLOCKED IT.** `resolvedBriefFor` + `briefFactsFor` are the gate's own two steps, extracted and shared by the gate, the completion response and the recovery. `missing[0]` IS `nextBriefFact`'s definition, so WHICH outstanding fact is surfaced is never decided in the route and never in the browser. ⚠️ **NO OUTSTANDING FACT ⇒ S1-RT-007's ORIGINAL DEMOTION, UNCHANGED.** A completion can be refused while holding all eleven (`icp: null` is the live case): there is nothing to tell the customer, the model's sentence is a genuine question wearing the wrong label, and it is delivered exactly as before. ⚠️ **AN UNREADABLE DURABLE BRIEF KEEPS ITS EXISTING FAIL-CLOSED BEHAVIOUR.** We do not know what is missing, so we do not say: no inference, no fabricated recovery truth, no completion, and **the customer is never asked to re-enter a fact because OUR read failed.** ⚠️ **AND THE PROMPT NOW CARRIES THE OUTSTANDING HALF** — guidance, never authority. The draft block listed what is HELD and never what is MISSING, so the model had to derive the gap itself; that subtraction failing is exactly what Cedar Peak looks like. The gate still refuses a short completion whatever the prompt says. | *"DO NOT make a second corrective LLM/model call."* · *"the invalid premature-completion sentence is NOT shown to the customer"* · *"The fallback recovery UI is NOT Milla speaking. It must be presented as UI/product state, not fake assistant dialogue."* · *"Do not show: 'Great, that's everything I need…' followed by: 'Actually, Exclusions are still needed.' That contradictory UX is forbidden for this fix."* · *"Do NOT make the portal own the eleven-fact authority."* | founder-locked 16 Sep | `routes/icps.ts` (`resolvedBriefFor` · `briefFactsFor` — the gate's steps, shared · the premature-completion recovery · the outstanding half of the draft prompt block) · `packages/shared/src/brief-facts.ts` (**unchanged** — `BRIEF_FACTS`, `BRIEF_FACT_LABEL`, `briefDraftFacts`) · `apps/portal/.../milla/welcome/page.tsx` (the `outstanding` reply branch — appends NOTHING to the transcript · the `role="status"` notice above an unchanged composer) · `s1-rt-010-premature-completion.test.ts` (15 assertions, **RED 9/15** pre-fix, 3 teeth proved) · ⛓️ **AR21** · **AR17** |
| **AR23** | 🛑 **THE CLIENT'S OWN COUNTRY IS ACCOUNT READINESS — IT IS NOT A TWELFTH BRIEF FACT (16 Sep, founder-locked, S1-ONB-001).** ⚠️ **TWO CLASSES, ONE ANSWER, ELEVEN FACTS.** `BRIEF_FACTS` is **eleven and stays eleven** — `packages/shared/src/brief-facts.ts` is not touched by this ruling and says by name that `country` and `phone` are **not** Brief facts. The client's own *"Based in"* is a separate **ACCOUNT** requirement, held in its own list (`ACCOUNT_FACTS`) with its own label map, and onboarding is `ready` only when **both** classes are satisfied. ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED IT.** `onboardSchema` refuses an account without a country, `clients.country` **DEFAULTS TO `'South Africa'`** — a silent placeholder the founder ruled out by name — and `/auth/onboard` sits **downstream of `confirmed_at`. So a Brief could be stamped CONFIRMED and only then be refused an account**, and the customer met the disagreement on screen: a finished targeting plan with a live *"Confirm my brief"* button sitting above the line **"Based in — still needed"**. ⚠️ **TARGET GEOGRAPHY MUST NEVER INFER IT.** Where a client's buyers are and where the client's own business is are different facts about different companies; `resolveBriefFacts` resolves `country` from `profile.country` then `brief_so_far.country` and from **nothing else** — `geographies` is never consulted, and a fallback from it would fabricate an account country from a market list. ⚠️ **PHONE IS OPTIONAL AND IS NEVER READINESS.** It is optional in `onboardSchema`, the prompt calls it *"genuinely optional"*, and it is the field a future human handoff will reuse; it is carried and persisted, never counted. ⚠️ **AND THE COUNTS STAY SEPARATE (S1-ONB-002).** Canonical Brief progress is the eleven with a matched denominator; the account gap is its own number and is **never folded into `remaining`** — mixing them told a customer *"12 things still needed"* about an eleven-fact Brief and under-reported held facts by one to the operator. | *"the client's own company country / \"Based in\" is an ACCOUNT-READINESS requirement for clean onboarding"* · *"it is NOT one of the canonical eleven Brief facts"* · *"target geography MUST NOT be used to infer the client's own country"* · *"phone remains optional and is not part of readiness"* | founder-locked 16 Sep | `lib/onboarding-state.ts` (`ACCOUNT_FACTS` · `ACCOUNT_FACT_LABEL` · `onboardingState` — the one readiness authority) · `packages/shared/src/brief-facts.ts` (**unchanged** — still eleven) · `lib/brief-fact-resolution.ts` (`country` from `profile.country` ?? `brief_so_far.country`, never `geographies`) · `routes/icps.ts` · `routes/milla.ts` · `apps/portal/.../milla/welcome/page.tsx` · `s1-onb-001-clean-onboarding.test.ts` (39 assertions, 3 teeth proved) · ⛓️ **AR22** · **R70** |
| **AR24** | ⛓️ **AMENDED 19 Sep — THE FOUNDER REVERSED THE OPERATIVE HALF OF THIS RULE, ON PRODUCTION EVIDENCE. LATEST WINS; THE ORIGINAL IS KEPT BELOW, STRUCK, BECAUSE ITS REASONING IS STILL TRUE AND IS ANSWERED ELSEWHERE.** 🛑 **THE CURRENT RULE: A FAILED DURABLE WRITE ALERTS US AND NEVER SILENCES MILLA.** When `saveBriefDraft` refuses (`promoted`, `unstorable`, `unverifiable`) or throws, the turn **completes normally**, the client carries on exactly as they did before 16 Sep, and the failure is raised as a **founder alert + a `brief_write_failed` Vida task**, deduped per (reason, user). ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED THE REVERSAL, AND THE FOUNDER COUNTED FIFTY-TWO.** The live log line is the whole diagnosis — `[icps/builder/chat] unusable model reply — {"category":"INVALID_SHAPE","stop_reason":"tool_use","input_key_count":5,"zod_paths":[]}`. **`zod_paths: []` means the reply VALIDATED**: Milla understood the client perfectly and produced a well-formed complete Brief. The only code that can null a validated completion is `mustNotConfirm`, reached because this rule's `heldWritable` went false. So a good answer was discarded and the client was shown *"Milla didn't catch that — just try again in a moment."* **The retry re-posted the same conversation into the same deterministic refusal; it could never succeed**, and each occurrence looked like a fresh transient hiccup because eight refusals share that one sentence. **Not one client was protected by failing closed, because no client ever got past it.** ⚠️ **AND NONE OF THE THREE REFUSALS IS THE CLIENT'S DOING.** `promoted` = the draft is sealed because the client is already real and their targeting lives on `clients`+`icps` — a **better** home than the draft; `unstorable` = the upsert errored, most often `20260911_onboarding_brief_drafts` never applied; `unverifiable` = the row could not be read first. ⚠️ **THE DANGER THE ORIGINAL NAMED IS ANSWERED, NOT DENIED.** Promotion without a draft invents no targeting: `/auth/onboard` stands aside when `briefDraftFor` answers null (`auth.ts:252`), the draft override at `POST /icps` stands aside, and the browser's own four legs create the client exactly as they did before drafts existed — **the path every client this product has ever had was created through**. ⚠️ **AND THE SILENCE IT HATED IS FIXED PROPERLY.** The pre-16-Sep degradation was silent; that was its real defect, and it is the half that is NOT restored. ⚠️ **WHAT IS UNCHANGED:** `held` is still advanced **only** on a successful save, so a failed write still grants **no fact authority** — the gate simply no longer refuses on it; nothing is fabricated; no write is reported as succeeding; **no second provider call**; and the **UNREADABLE** case (S1-RT-009) is **byte-unchanged** — not knowing what a client told us is a different thing from knowing and failing to keep a copy. ⚠️ **SECOND DOOR, SAME RULE (19 Sep).** `POST /milla/brief-draft/confirm` answered `unstorable` with **503-retryable**, and that reason covered *"the draft store could not be READ at all"* — so fixing only the chat would have moved the wall to the Confirm click. The read-side failure is now its own reason, `store_unavailable`, answered **404** so the portal's existing tolerance carries the client through the pre-draft path (its own comment already said so: *"a client whose draft predates this table, **or whose draft could not be stored**, has nothing to confirm"*). **Narrow on purpose:** `unstorable` still means the row is right there and the stamp would not write — a real transient where a retry can succeed — and `incomplete` / `unsupported_geography` are product refusals and are **unmoved**. ~~**[STRUCK 19 Sep — the original rule, kept for its reasoning:]** 🛑 **NO DURABLE WRITE = NO NEW FACT AUTHORITY = NO READY ADVANCEMENT (16 Sep, founder-locked, S1-ONB-003).**~~ ⚠️ **THE RULE.** A model may understand a customer perfectly; if that accepted truth did not reach **canonical persisted memory**, the turn **must not advance onboarding on it**. Readiness after the write is driven by what was actually kept — never by a value that exists only in this turn's reply. ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED IT, and it was reachable in one turn.** `held` was correctly withheld on a failed save, but the completion gate does not count `held` — it counts the **resolution**, the record unioned with this turn's reply. That union is right while the write lands (the write stores the same projection) and wrong when it fails: the **last missing fact could satisfy the gate from the reply alone and reach no database**, producing `type: 'complete'`, `onboarding_state: 'ready'` and a plan to Confirm. The client's next act is CONFIRM, which **promotes the draft** — they would approve a plan their Brief cannot honour. ⚠️ **IT IS THE WRITE-SIDE TWIN OF THE UNREADABLE RULE, AND THE TWO SPLIT ON THE SENTENCE.** *Unreadable* (S1-RT-009) — we do not know what they told us, so a completion is **demoted to the conversation** and Milla's own sentence carries the turn; **unchanged**. *Unwriteable* (this) — we know exactly what they told us and failed to keep it, so it is an **HONEST SYSTEM FAILURE**: a retryable error, nothing attributed to Milla. Demoting instead would deliver a sentence written to CLOSE the conversation as the turn meant to CONTINUE it (the AR22 stranding), and the AR22 recovery is equally wrong here because it would name an outstanding fact **the customer has already given**. ⚠️ **NOTHING IS PRETENDED, FABRICATED OR RE-ROLLED.** No write is reported as succeeding, no default or blank is invented, and there is **no second provider call** — one turn, one request. The portal keeps the client's turn and re-sends the history, and the next save merges, so the fact lands then. ⚠️ **IT IS UNCONDITIONAL, AND IT SUPERSEDES THE OLD DEGRADATION (founder-locked 16 Sep, S1-ONB-004).** ⛓️ The first cut scoped this to a canonical record that had actually been read (`held` non-empty) and reported the wider case as the founder's to decide; he decided: **FAIL CLOSED UNIVERSALLY.** A completion is refused whether an existing readable draft was being extended **or the draft store is wholly unavailable and no durable record can be established at all** — the previous *"best-effort degradation may still complete without a durable draft"* behaviour is **retired**. The two states are indistinguishable to the person on the screen: a finished plan with a live *"Confirm my brief"* is one click from **PROMOTION**, and a promotion whose Brief was never written is a client whose targeting exists nowhere. ⚠️ **SCOPE IS ADVANCEMENT, NOT CONVERSATION.** The hard boundary is **DECLARED COMPLETE / READY + NO SUCCESSFUL DURABLE CANONICAL WRITE = FAIL CLOSED** — no plan, no Confirm, no promotion, no fabricated success. A question turn still answers in Milla's own words through a wholly unavailable store, and onboarding is never turned into a form. | ⛓️ **19 Sep, on the proposal *"if the notebook write fails, we alert you and let the client carry on — the way it worked before"*:** *"yes agreed."* · *"that is the same error 52 times now."* · *"we have had this failure many times… and i mean at least 50"* · *"try again does not help"* · **[16 Sep, superseded]** *"NO DURABLE WRITE = NO NEW FACT AUTHORITY = NO READY ADVANCEMENT."* · *"The model may understand something correctly. But if accepted truth did not successfully reach canonical persisted memory, that turn must not advance onboarding based on that transient value."* · *"Do NOT silently pretend persistence succeeded."* · *"`held` after the persistence attempt is the only truth allowed to drive post-write readiness."* | founder-locked 16 Sep · ⛓️ **amended 19 Sep (latest wins)** | **CURRENT (19 Sep):** `routes/icps.ts` (`heldWritable` **deleted** · `mustNotConfirm = !heldReadable && declaredType === 'complete'` · a failed or **thrown** save logs `durable brief not written`, raises `brief_write_failed` fire-and-forget, and continues · the unreadable branch **byte-unchanged**) · `routes/milla.ts` (`store_unavailable` → 404 + alert; `unstorable` still 503-retryable) · `lib/brief-draft.ts` (`confirmBriefDraft` read-side failure split out as `store_unavailable`; **`saveBriefDraft` untouched**) · `lib/alerts.ts` + `lib/operator-tasks.ts` (`brief_write_failed`, severity `warn`, deduped per reason+user) · `s1-onb-003-persistence-authority.test.ts` (**re-judged, not deleted** — 30 assertions, **RED 20/30 against the real pre-fix source**, controls held) · `confirm-store-unavailable.test.ts` (7, the second door) · `first-run-milla.test.ts` · `s1-runtime-batch.test.ts` (string pins re-judged, claims unchanged) · **[16 Sep, superseded]** `routes/icps.ts` (`heldWritable` — set on any `SaveOutcome` failure where a record existed · `mustNotConfirm = (!heldReadable \|\| !heldWritable) && declaredType === 'complete'`) · `s1-onb-003-persistence-authority.test.ts` (22 assertions, **RED 5/12** pre-fix) · ⛓️ **AR22** · **AR23** |

## 10 · MORE SAFETY, PROCESS & OPERATIONS

## 11 · REPORTING — the standings report is a LOCKED FORMAT *(founder-locked 6 Aug)*

> **What earned this.** On 6 Aug the morning standings report had one shape and the evening one had another — sections renamed, tables missing their Owner and When columns, items dropped between reports. The founder: *"i need a full detailed breakwon. and here i am seeing 2 columns with no owner or timeline either. this is not good enough. and we working one way at one point and changing another. again. we have rules in place… i cant trust the docs at the moment. and i cant trust this source. so give me a way to work."* Then, on the proposed rules: **"lock this."** A report whose shape drifts is a report the founder has to re-learn how to read every time — which is the same defect as a doc that drifts.

| # | Rule | The founder's words | Source | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **RPT1** | **One report shape, forever.** The standings report is ALWAYS the same template — numbered sections (✅ shipped & closed · 🔴 critical band · 🚶 walks owed · 🙋 rulings queue · 👁 watch · ⚪ open-not-blocking · 📅 dates · bottom line), and **every table ends in Owner · When. No exceptions, no redesigns.** A report missing either column is defective and the founder should reject it on sight. | *"lock this."* | 6 Aug | nothing — words only, so every model must re-read this section before writing a standings report (the citation law) |
| **RPT2** | **The report states what is on `main` versus what is waiting on the founder** — two board lines. Nothing pushed counts as done until his merge, and no report may lead with "merge this" ahead of the report itself. | *"im not merging anything"* (6 Aug, after a report led with a merge ask) | 6 Aug | nothing — words only |
| **RPT3** | **No item ever silently disappears between reports.** Anything in the previous report appears in the next — closed, moved, or still open — until the founder says drop it. | *"there is a lot more items sorry"* | 6 Aug | nothing — words only |
| **RPT4** | **The founder's saved prompt below IS the request** — when he pastes it, RPT1–RPT3 bind the answer, and every number in it is verified live (`git fetch` before any ref, `list_pull_requests` before naming any PR, counts run fresh) — nothing from memory, per the working method. | — | 6 Aug | this row is what the prompt cites |

**THE SAVED PROMPT (RPT4) — the founder pastes exactly this:**

```
FULL STANDINGS REPORT — locked format, register §11 (RPT1–RPT4, 6 Aug).
Sections 1–9, every table ends Owner · When. Two board lines: on main vs
awaiting my merge. Carry every item from the previous report — closed, moved,
or open. Verify everything live before writing: git fetch, real counts, PR
states via list_pull_requests. Nothing from memory. End with the bottom line
and my next steps.
```

## 15 · THE 19 AUGUST RULINGS *(captured same-session, ritual 4b)*

> **Five** rulings from the day the founder ran his own dashboard checks and found what no test we own had asked. ⛓️ *This line said "Four" until R49 was added the same evening, after Fable reviewed the full GPT handoff bundle — corrected rather than left to drift, because a count that stops being true is how a page stops being read.* Two of them (R46, R47) were made on **17 Aug** and are logged late — recorded here with that gap stated rather than back-dated, because a ruling that lived only in a chat transcript for two days is exactly what this page exists to prevent.

| # | Rule | The founder's words | Source | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**R45**~~ ⛓️ | ⛓️ **SUPERSEDED 20 Aug BY R54 — SOUTH AFRICA IS NOT CONDITIONAL.** This row recorded SA as joining *"via the POPIA s69 consent-request route once that route exists in code and counsel confirms the reading"*. The founder corrected that reading on 20 Aug: **the launch list is US, UK and South Africa**, and expanding to further countries is a dial he turns when he chooses. His words: *"no my launch rule is US, UK and South Africa"* · *"South Africa is on the list and shold i choose to expand when i want to other countries i go. nothing stops. we compliant though."* ⚠️ **The condition this row attached was mine, not his** — his 19-Aug words were *"we may be South Africa too. only reason. i have a partner there who wants to start month end"*, and the s69 conditionality was added around them. That is the shape the Citation Law exists to catch, and it took five days and a contradiction to surface. **F17 asks Fable to confirm nothing in the compliance position rests on the condition this row invented.** *(Original kept below per the chain rule.)* 🌍 **LAUNCH COUNTRIES — FIRST SEND IS US + UK.** EU and Middle East leads are **HELD** until counsel answers L1 and a country gate exists. **South Africa is CONDITIONAL:** it joins via the POPIA s69 consent-request route once that route exists in code and counsel confirms the reading — the SA partner starting month-end **onboards clients now**; SA **cold outreach waits**. ⛓️ Narrows **R43** (the book is global) for the launch window only: R43 fixes the jurisdictions we intend to reach, this fixes which ones we may send to on day one. ⚠️ **The book and the ruling do not currently meet:** of 166 real leads, **zero** carry a US or UK country and **166 of 166 carry no country at all** (counted live 19 Aug from the founder's own export), so under this ruling the entire book is held until US/UK leads are sourced. | *"i agree with launch countries."* · *"we may be South Africa too. only reason. i have a partner there who wants to start month end."* | 19 Aug | ✅ **BUILT 20 Aug — see R50 and item #663.** `LAUNCH_SEND_COUNTRIES` in `@kind/shared` · six gates (approve-lead step 3d before the $4 · two enrol routes · `autoEnrollLead` · day-1 batch · `sendSequenceEmail`) · plus a front-door fence on the ICP target list. `lib/pecr.ts` is UNTOUCHED and still allows unknown-country through — correctly: the launch gate is an additional layer that inverts that call on its own commercial grounds, not a change to the legal test |
| **R46** | 📁 **VIDA IS THE SINGLE HOME FOR GOVERNED DOCUMENTS.** Anything that must be created, held or governed lives in the Vida operator console as the sole source of truth. ⚠️ **Logged late** — ruled 17 Aug, written down 19 Aug. ⛓️ The Vida **CONSOLE** stays internal forever (**R36**), so whatever this becomes is operator-only and never client-facing. | *"any documents we need to create hold of be governed get held in Vida operator. on sole truth of source."* | 17 Aug, logged 19 Aug | ✅ **BUILT 20 Aug (#678)** — `governed_documents` (migration `20260820_governed_documents`, both homes, runner 27→**28**) · `apps/admin/src/app/governed-documents` · three operator routes · `writeOperatorAudit` on every write. **Versions CHAIN and nothing deletes:** no UPDATE, PUT, PATCH or DELETE route exists, no delete control on the screen, and no `governed_document_deleted` audit action to record one with — all three asserted as absences. | **R66** | 🧪 **PAID TESTING FREEZE — NO LIVE PROVIDER SEARCH MAY BE SPENT MERELY TO TEST PRODUCT BEHAVIOUR (25 Aug).** Launch testing runs on **mocked providers or existing pooled contacts**, and nothing else. ⚠️ **The specific failure this closes: a pool-backed test must NOT silently fall through to PDL/Apollo/Hunter when the pool is thin.** Today's `runIcpJob` does exactly that — `servePoolLeads` returns what it can and the PDL remainder is bought without anyone asking, which is how a *"free"* flow test spends real acquisition budget. ⚠️ **The test contacts do not need to be a good fit.** What is being proved is FLOW and SAFETY — surfacing, terminal states, acceptance, boundaries — not targeting quality, and demanding realistic matches is what pushes a tester back to a live search. ⛓️ Chains AR17/AR18 (the 40-record and $300 fences remain the money ceiling; this rule stops us reaching them for a reason that was never worth it). | *"No further launch testing may use live paid provider searches merely to test product behaviour."* · *"The test contacts do not need to be perfect fit; flow and safety are what are being proven."* | 25 Aug | ✅ **BUILT 26 Aug — `SAFE_TEST_MODE` blocks every paid provider AT the `fetch` boundary** (`lib/paid-provider-guard.ts`): PDL (`pdl-search.ts` ×2, `enrichment.ts`), Apollo (`apollo.ts` ×3), Hunter (×2) and Clearbit. **It THROWS, never returns empty** — an exhausted safe pool must not look like a finished audience. ⚠️ **CORRECTED ON REVIEW 26 Aug — the first version relied on a human remembering `SAFE_TEST_MODE`, and NOTHING in the repo set it.** Now **fail-closed**: paid providers are OFF unless `PAID_PROVIDERS_ENABLED=true` is set deliberately, so forgetting a variable costs a refused call rather than money. | **R67** | 💾 **RETENTION IS NOT CONTACTABILITY — EVERY PAID PROVIDER RECORD IS REMEMBERED, AND DNC STILL NEVER GETS CONTACTED (25 Aug).** Every record K.I.N.D pays a provider to acquire must be retained as company acquisition memory **before client-specific rejection can destroy the asset**. Two logical states, and they are not the same table's job: **① CONTACTABLE INVENTORY** — may be reused for another client if eligible. **② NON-CONTACTABLE ACQUISITION MEMORY** — DNC, opted-out and suppressed identities, retained so K.I.N.D remembers it has seen and paid for them and never treats them as unknown again, and **never** so they can be contacted. Requirements: provider identity · provider provenance · original acquisition cost · **emailless records where a provider identity exists** · client A's rejection must not destroy the asset · client B may use contactable inventory later only if eligible · **suppression/opt-out/DNC always wins over serving and contact** · duplicate storage must not double-count acquisition cost. ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED THIS, verified live 25 Aug:** a proof run reserved 20 records, PDL returned people, and `total_inserted: 0` — the pool write sits BELOW every client gate (`icps.ts:985`), so a record dropped for *this client's* budget cap or *this client's* duplicate is thrown away as company inventory. ⚠️ **AND THE PART THAT IS NOT SOLVED, recorded so it is never assumed:** remembering a person does **NOT** stop PDL billing for them again. The shipped query has no exclusion clause — `buildPdlBody` emits `bool.must` only, zero `must_not` — so PDL decides what to return before we see it. **Cost avoidance from re-acquisition is launch-critical RESEARCH and must not be marked solved without evidence** that a provider-id exclusion is supported and safe. ⛓️ Chains F13/F15 (cross-client reuse is a licensing question counsel still owns) and the `POOL_ELIGIBLE_SOURCES` tripwire, which this rule does not widen. | *"Every record K.I.N.D pays to acquire from a provider must be retained as company acquisition memory/inventory before client-specific rejection can destroy the asset."* · *"RETENTION != CONTACTABILITY."* · *"Do NOT claim that remembering a record automatically prevents PDL billing again."* | 25 Aug | ✅ **BUILT 26 Aug — `public.acquisition_memory`**, written in `runIcpJob` **BEFORE** all six gates that used to discard paid contacts (budget cap · DNC · opt-out · this-client duplicate · insert failure · no email). Keyed `(source, provider_id)` so **emailless paid identities are retained** — `lead_pool.email_norm` is its PRIMARY KEY and structurally cannot hold them. ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING ⇒ acquisition cost written once, never double-counted. Suppressed/opt-out/DNC rows are **remembered and marked uncontactable**; nothing in the send, consent, reveal, scoring or serving path reads the table. RLS ON with **no policies**. ⚠️ **CORRECTED ON REVIEW 26 Aug — two defects found before merge.** ① The write was **non-fatal**, so a failed memory write let the run walk on into the gates and lose a paid identity silently; it now **fails closed** (one retry, then the run stops). ② `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` alone left **stale `contactable = true`** when an identity later turned up suppressed; a second narrow UPDATE now **tightens contactability only**, never loosens it, and never touches cost or timestamps. ⚠️ **STILL OPEN: this does NOT prove a provider will not rebill** — `buildPdlBody` still emits zero `must_not`. |

approval** instead of thousands of paid-lead approvals, execution in **controlled ~250-lead batches** that auto-continue when healthy and auto-pause on a material problem, a client **Pause Programme** control that stops sourcing AND sending, **material ICP change auto-pausing** future sourcing until reconfirmed, **no sourcing or spend outside explicit programme authority**, **unused programme value that never expires**, the first 50% **non-refundable once sourcing is authorised** with the second 50% **not charged if paused before Go Live**, and **making the client whole** for authorised value K.I.N.D cannot deliver. ⚠️ **NONE OF THIS IS BUILT — BUT IT IS THE INTENDED ARCHITECTURE, NOT AN OPTION AWAITING A DECISION.** Read this rule in three registers and never collapse them: **LIVE NOW (legacy runtime)** = the 3 Aug model, **$299 pack · first 100 approvals included · $4 per approved lead** (`PACK_LEADS` · `PACK_PRICE_USD` · `LEAD_PRICE_USD` in `@kind/shared`), operational and unchanged · **SUPERSEDED HISTORY** = **R68**'s $4→$8 migration (LAUNCH-PAD **T9**), an earlier future direction now overtaken, preserved not deleted · **CURRENT DIRECTION** = this rule. **Legacy runtime remains operational until the coordinated programme migration is built, tested, founder-approved and deployed.** ⚠️ **A future agent must not treat the legacy commercial architecture as the intended product**, and nothing here may be quoted to a client, a partner or the website until it ships. ⛓️ **IT SUPERSEDES FLAT-$4 AS THE INTENDED ARCHITECTURE WITHOUT DELETING IT AS TODAY'S RUNTIME** — and the earlier 27 Aug instruction *"do not change pricing yet"* was a build boundary on PR1A/PR2, not a decision to keep flat-$4 permanently. Both stand, in different registers: do not change what runs, and what runs is not the destination. ⚠️ **TWO THINGS REMAIN GENUINELY OPEN, AND THEY ARE THE FOUNDER'S TO SETTLE — the rest are chained supersessions, not conflicts:** ① **THE BENCHMARK.** The programme seed of **250 leads per targeted booked meeting** against **R69**, which locks the centre case at **~150 accepted prospects per booked meeting** (range 100–250) and makes **250–300 with no booked meeting a campaign-review trigger**. The denominators were checked, not assumed: R69 counts *accepted/contacted prospects*, the seed counts *recommended programme leads* — different stages, **except that this rule's own 1:1 commercial sourcing assumption collapses them onto the same denominator**. > The three blockers that stood between the reconciliation and an operating system. We had graphs and colours. This feels very shallow. One shared header, two customer emails, the same invented number; a fix applied to the digest alone would have left the identical figure shipping from the same function **and passed a guard while doing it**. So the value was removed from the header's SIGNATURE, not from a call site. ⛓️ **FOUNDER-RULED: NOTHING REPLACES IT** — *"Do NOT replace it with another invented metric, invent deal value, invent revenue, invent ROI, or use score × 100 as money."* The stat row is now two real cells (a count and a score). ⚠️ **THE `scoring.ts:221` WRITER REMAINS PARKED AS ITS OWN DEFECT** — this removes the customer-facing money claim and does not pretend to have fixed the column. **The lesson to carry: a parked defect can have exits nobody has enumerated.** This one reached two inboxes, not just the ROI page it was found on. *(Original row below, unchanged and still in force.)* 🔔 **A CONTROL A CUSTOMER CAN SEE MUST MATCH WHAT THE PRODUCT ACTUALLY DOES — IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (31 Aug).** A switch that claims LESS than the product does is the same defect as one that claims more. ⚠️ **WHAT EARNED IT.** The Milla Settings notification panel badged **"Soon"** on three rows with DISABLED switches while their crons ran: `/digest/weekly` every Monday and `/figsy/check-performance` every morning. **#326 wrote "Soon" when those toggles genuinely did nothing** — they wrote localStorage and nothing read it. **The emails were built afterwards and nobody came back to the switch.** A client was receiving mail the product told them did not exist, from a control they could see and could not press. **This is #628's shape exactly — the copy outlived the problem and became the opposite lie.** ⚠️ **AND THE STRUCTURAL CAUSE, WHICH IS THE REUSABLE PART: A PREFERENCE A CRON MUST OBEY CANNOT LIVE IN A BROWSER.** All four original toggles were localStorage, which is precisely why none of them worked. `daily_brief_enabled` was already a column for this reason (R2/#27). **Any notification switch shown to a customer is a server column or it is not a switch.** **FOUNDER DECISIONS, 31 Aug (D1):** **Low credits** — *removed from the Milla programme experience*; programme customers must not receive retired low-credit emails; the panel itself is preserved with no awkward gap. **Campaign paused** — KEEP, made genuinely live, switchable and persisted; **NOT renamed to "Programme paused"** unless the underlying event genuinely means the whole programme stopped (it does not — it is one `figsy_campaigns` row crossing the <1% reply-rate floor; a programme pause is `programmes.paused_at`). **Weekly digest** — KEEP, live, switchable, persisted. **Reply received** — stays "Soon", because it genuinely is. **Daily brief** — unchanged. | *"Remove Low credits from the Milla programme experience."* · *"Milla programme customers must not receive retired low-credit emails."* · *"Preserve the notification panel itself. Do not leave an empty/awkward visual gap."* · *"Do NOT automatically rename to 'Programme paused' unless the underlying event genuinely means the full programme is paused."* | 31 Aug | **BUILD-004A-2D** · `lib/programme-notifications.ts` · `20260831_notification_prefs_and_referral_handoff` · `milla-settings-referral.test.ts` · ⛓️ R2/#27 · #326 · #628 |
| **R88** | 🤝 **MVP REFERRAL IS HUMAN-HANDLED — NO AUTOMATIC MONETARY REWARD (31 Aug).** The Referral **experience is KEPT** — page, visual structure, link, copy action, referral history. What is removed from the Milla journey is **every retired commercial figure**: no **$45** promise · no **wallet** · no **11 approved leads** · no **$299 onboarding** · no **credits** · **no automatic monetary reward**. **The backend automatic $45 wallet credit must NOT execute for the new Milla referral journey**, and **historical referral evidence is not deleted**. **No new reward scheme may be invented** — if a replacement needs a genuinely new commercial promise or CTA behaviour, **STOP for founder approval.** ⚠️ **THE FINDING WAS NOT THAT THE PAGE LIED.** Every reward sentence was TRUE against the backend: `stripe.ts` really credited $45, and $45 ÷ $4 really is 11 approved leads. **The defect was that `/milla/billing` — two rail items away — tells the same customer there is no wallet, no pack and no per-lead price.** Both pages were truthful about different halves of a product mid-migration (**R74** keeps the legacy runtime live). **Truth per-page is not truth per-customer.** ⚠️ **AND THE TRAP INSIDE THE FIX, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-DISCOVERED:** the refund path claws $45 back whenever `clients.referral_bonus_paid_at` is set. **A retired payout must never reuse that marker** — marking an unpaid referral with it makes the first refund reclaim money nobody was given, from a wallet the customer's own billing page says does not exist. The handoff therefore claims a **separate** `referral_handoff_at`, and historic paid referrals keep reversing correctly. | *"MVP REFERRAL MODEL: HUMAN-HANDLED."* · *"no automatic monetary reward"* · *"The existing backend automatic $45 wallet-credit side effect must NOT execute for the new Milla referral journey."* · *"Do not delete historical referral evidence."* · *"Do not invent a new reward scheme."* | 31 Aug | **BUILD-004A-2D** · `routes/stripe.ts` (`handOffReferralToFounder`) · `clients.referral_handoff_at` · ⛓️ **R74** (legacy runtime stays live) · #336 · #349 · #607 |

The area becomes **truthful programme delivery information from real programme data**. ⚠️ ***"Do not replace useful UI with a sentence"*** — and **before inventing any NEW visible metric, label, value, control or promise, STOP for founder approval.** *(Both controls were levers on our own machinery: `leads_per_run` feeds `sourcingTarget()`, `daily_drip_rate` feeds the nightly `/leads/drip` cron — and for a programme client the second is not even reachable, because `start-work.ts` stamps `delivered_at` at surfacing so their leads never trickle.)* **D4 · FIGSY NAMING.** **Milla = the customer experience; FIGSY = the engine behind Milla.** **KEEP FIGSY customer-visible where it genuinely refers to the engine doing research, sourcing, scoring or outreach.** ⚠️ **NO GLOBAL RENAME.** An individual FIGSY reference changes **only if it is technically wrong in that exact context.** | *"KEEP the Lead Delivery section and its visual treatment. Do NOT strip the cards."* · *"Customers must NOT control M&V internal sourcing quantity or internal delivery mechanics."* · *"Do not replace useful UI with a sentence."* · *"Milla = customer experience, FIGSY = engine behind Milla."* · *"Do NOT globally rename FIGSY to Milla."* | 31 Aug | **BUILD-004A-2D** · `(milla)/milla/settings/page.tsx` · `/my/programme` (`readCustomerProgramme`) · ⛓️ **R86** |
| **R85** | 🧭 **GITHUB HOLDS THE LIVE ITEM RECORD — THE REPO STILL HOLDS CANONICAL TRUTH.** | *"GitHub becomes the live status/inventory for ITEMS."* · *"Canonical assets remain real repository files."* | Founder ruling, 29 Aug | The K.I.N.D GitHub Project + `kind-inventory` issues |

**R85, in full:**

1. **K.I.N.D has two operating layers: Company Brain and Product System.**
2. **Canonical assets remain real repository files and artifacts.** They are sources of truth, **not tasks**.
3. **A required change to a canonical asset becomes a GitHub item linked to that asset** — the asset is never converted into a task.
4. **GitHub Issues are the live records for actionable items.**
5. **The K.I.N.D GitHub Project is the live operating/status surface** for those items.
6. **An item may belong to Company Brain, Product System, or Both.**
7. **Legacy backlog/status sources — including `PRODUCT-INVENTORY.md`, `V2-TRACKER.md`, `LAUNCH-PAD.md` and the roadmaps — are preserved as evidence and history once retired, and must not continue as competing live status systems.**
8. ⚠️ **They are NOT retired yet.** Retirement requires the GitHub Project to exist.
9. **Conflicts become Review/Reconcile items. Unknown remains unknown. Historical evidence is preserved.**

⛓️ **This amends, and does not delete, the earlier single-status rule.** `CLAUDE.md`'s *"STATUS lives only in PRODUCT-INVENTORY"* and Protocol rule 22 (*no new current-truth surface without an explicit rule change*) were written before this ruling and stand as history; **R85 is the explicit rule change they required.** Chained per R82, latest wins.

---

## 19 · THE 3 SEPTEMBER RULINGS — CURRENT WORK IS A ROW FACT *(captured same-session, ritual 4b)*

> Three attempts were made to stop House rendering its retired per-lead desk as a current Milla workspace, and each shipped green. The founder stopped the loop and ruled on what a boundary may be made of. These rows exist because the second attempt's mistake was **invisible to every automated check** — the mixed-history case that breaks it had no test.

| # | Rule | The founder's words | Source | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R90** | 🛑 **CUMULATIVE ACCOUNT STATE IS NOT ROW-LEVEL ATTRIBUTION — AND `proof_passes_done` IS WITHDRAWN AS A WORKSPACE BOUNDARY (3 Sep, founder-locked).** A counter on `clients` answers *"has this account EVER claimed a free-proof pass"*. It **cannot** answer *"does THIS LEAD belong to the current M&V Free Proof experience"*, and using one for the other reopened the whole defect: an explicit programme-model client with old NULL-programme legacy leads who later runs a legitimate modern Free Proof gets `proof_passes_done > 0` → the desk unbounds → **every historical legacy card is current again.** ⚠️ **`proof_started_at` IS ALSO REFUSED** — it is rewritten on every claim, so bounding by it hides pass 1 from a client on pass 2, against the founder-locked *"two labelled proof sets, nothing deleted or filtered away"*. ⚠️ **AND SO IS EVERY OTHER SHORTCUT, NAMED:** company name · House client id · calendar cutoff · `commercial_model` alone · `proof_passes_done` alone · `proof_started_at` alone · surfaced timestamp alone · `programme_id IS NULL` alone · deletion · blanket backfill. **What the boundary must be: a positive fact stored on the row the run created.** ⛓️ **This is the SAME error class as C2** — *"no programme ⇒ legacy"* had simply become *"ever proofed ⇒ show everything"*. **An absence is never a positive fact, whichever column it is an absence in.** | *"proof_passes_done is cumulative historical client state."* · *"It does NOT necessarily answer 'Does this particular lead belong to the legitimate current M&V Free Proof experience?'"* · *"That would recreate the same defect after we thought we fixed it."* · *"If proof_started_at is unsuitable because it rewrites each pass, do not revive it blindly."* | 3 Sep | **PR #1636** · `leads.proof_pass` (`20260903_lead_proof_attribution`) · `lib/current-workspace.ts` · `current-workspace.test.ts` ②③ · ⛓️ **C2** |
| **R91** | 🧪 **FREE PROOF IS CALIBRATION, NOT OUTREACH (3 Sep, founder-locked).** A client in Free Proof with no programme must show **replies = `[]` · campaign = `null` · meetings = none · outreach progress = 0 where appropriate.** Free proof sends nobody an email and books nobody, so **any campaign, reply or meeting readable for such a client is by construction an earlier motion's** — presenting it as current activity is the bleed itself. ⚠️ **THE ARGUMENT THAT FAILED, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT REPEATED:** *"free proof sends nothing, so the rail is empty in practice"* describes **the motion**, not **the account**. It is true of a clean new prospect and false of a declared programme client between programmes — which is exactly whose screenshot started this. ⚠️ **The proof scope may permit POSITIVELY-ATTRIBUTED PROOF CARDS ONLY.** | *"Free Proof is calibration, not outreach."* · *"proof scope may permit positively-attributed PROOF CARDS only."* · *"Prove the shared resolver cannot accidentally expose these simply because proof work exists."* | 3 Sep | **PR #1636** · `lib/milla-summary.ts` (`campaignFor`, `recentRepliesFor`, meeting counts) · `showsCurrentOutreach` · `proof-attribution-boundary.test.ts` ①④ |
| **R92** | 🧬 **A CLEAN SYNTHETIC FIXTURE IS NOT A TEST OF A MIXED-HISTORY DEFECT (3 Sep, founder-locked).** Where a fix turns on telling **two histories on ONE account** apart, the regression test must seed **both** and assert **both halves**: the modern records **ARE shown** AND the historical ones **are NOT**. ⚠️ **AN EMPTY RESULT IS A FAILURE, NOT A PASS** — a boundary that hides everything passes every "history must not appear" assertion ever written, which is precisely how three attempts shipped green. ⚠️ **AND THE NEW TEST MUST GO RED AGAINST THE IMPLEMENTATION IT REPLACES BEFORE IT GOES GREEN**, or it is not evidence that it tests the defect. ⛓️ **This is the general form of the lesson `check.sh` learned about doc-lint** — a suite verifies the shapes it was given, never whether a fixture describes the world. | *"A clean synthetic new client with no historical rows is NOT enough."* · *"Also prove the 2 modern cards are actually present. A result of an empty desk is NOT a pass."* · *"RED this test against the current withdrawn implementation first."* | 3 Sep | **PR #1636** · `apps/api/src/lib/proof-attribution-boundary.test.ts` (RED 21/36 against the withdrawn rule, then 36/36) |
| **R93** | ✅ **THE EXISTING-PROOF AUDIT GATE — RUN, ANSWERED, AND CLOSED (3 Sep, founder-ruled on PRODUCTION data).** `leads.proof_pass` is NULL on every row written before it existed, so a **legitimate** free-proof lead created earlier reads the same as retired history and the new reader hides both. That consequence was **not** guessed at and **not** backfilled away — the founder ran the read-only audit (`docs/audits/2026-09-03_existing_free_proof_audit.sql`) against production. **IT RETURNED EXACTLY THREE ROWS:** *Disrupt Shop* (4 null-programme awaiting cards) and *Glean* (4, and 20) — all three with `already_proof_attributed = 0`. 🛑 **HOUSE DID NOT APPEAR. MBF DID NOT APPEAR.** **THE RULING:** all three are **demo/test accounts, not live customer work**. Their rows **stay historical with `proof_pass = NULL`**, need **no backfill**, **may disappear** from the current Milla workspace, and must **not be deleted or modified**. ⚠️ **WHAT THIS ROW IS ACTUALLY FOR, AND IT IS NOT THE THREE NAMES.** It records that the migration's one open consequence was closed by **looking**, on the real database, before it could bite — rather than by a heuristic, a timestamp cutoff, a newest-N-batches inference or a blanket backfill, every one of which was available and every one of which would have manufactured provenance (R90). ⚠️ **AND THE GATE IS SPENT, NOT STANDING.** It answered a BEFORE-the-reader question and the reader has landed. Re-running the query now answers *what did* disappear rather than *what would*; the three rows above are the answer to both. A NEW account, a restored backup or a second attribution column re-opens the question, and this query is the instrument that answers it. | *"Founder context confirms Disrupt Shop and Glean were demo/test accounts, not live customer work."* · *"Those demo/test rows remain historical with proof_pass=NULL."* · *"They do NOT need backfill."* · *"They may disappear from the CURRENT Milla workspace."* · *"Do not delete or modify them."* · *"THE EXISTING-PROOF AUDIT GATE IS PASSED."* | 3 Sep · production audit | `docs/audits/2026-09-03_existing_free_proof_audit.sql` (the query and its result) · `20260903_lead_proof_attribution` · ⛓️ **R90** (no inference, no backfill) |
