| **R107** | 🛑 **NOBODY IS ASKED TO APPROVE WORK THAT DOES NOT EXIST — AND PROGRAMME AUTHORITY IS NOT PROVIDER MONEY (7 Sep, founder-locked, House delivery preparation package).** Two live defects on the same House programme, with one root shape between them: a fact was inferred from an absence. 🛑 **① VIDA OFFERED "Ready for approval" AGAINST NOTHING.** `markReadyForApproval` asked exactly one question — *does any lead carry this programme id, delivered and surfaced?* — which was the right guard for the defect it was written for (P1 → Ready with nothing sourced) and nowhere near sufficient for what the status MEANS: *a human may now look at what will run, and approve it.* There was no batch, no campaign, no sequence, no messaging, no cadence, no sender binding and no frozen review set, and **an approval collected against nothing is worse than no approval, because everybody downstream treats it as consent to send.** **THE RULE:** one canonical readiness rule (`lib/preparation-readiness.ts`), consulted **by the transition itself** and not merely by a screen — hiding the control in Vida would leave the API willing to make the transition, so UI and backend would disagree about what is allowed. It **refuses and names the missing piece**, every time, because the operator's next action depends entirely on WHICH one is missing; it **returns all blockers, not the first**; and it **grants nothing** — no P2, no Live, no enrolment, no send. ⚠️ **NOTHING WAS WEAKENED TO BUILD IT:** the old lead rule survives intact as one of fifteen, and the two existing tests that encoded the smaller rule were rewritten to the larger one rather than deleted. 🛑 **② A SUCCESSFUL HOUSE RUN WAS UNACCOUNTED FOR.** 246 people sourced; the programme read **`0 used · 0 reserved · 2500 left · no batch has been opened yet`**. `try_spend_sourcing` did TWO unrelated jobs in one body — programme **ENTITLEMENT** (reserve, ceiling, 250 batch cap) and PDL **MONEY** (a `sourcing_ledger` row at $0.28 a record). House sources from **prepaid Apollo**, so recording $0.28 a head would have been a fabricated cost; the 22-Aug fix therefore exempted House from the WHOLE function and lost the entitlement accounting with it — including `openBatch`, which sat in the same `else` branch. ⚠️ **THE COUNTERS WERE THE SYMPTOM; THE CEILING WAS THE DEFECT — nothing was enforcing the programme's 2,500 limit on the house path at all.** **THE RULE:** authority and cost are separate facts. `try_reserve_programme_sourcing` is the entitlement half, extracted whole and calling no ledger; `try_spend_sourcing` **keeps its signature** and calls it before writing its PDL row, so **one implementation of the ceiling serves both callers** and AR5/AR8 is untouched — House still never calls the cash fence. ⚠️ **AND THE RECORDS ALREADY DELIVERED ARE RECONCILED, NOT REWRITTEN:** `reconcile_programme_sourcing` is **operator-invoked for one named programme**, adds a settled batch and stamps the orphaned leads, **deletes nothing**, is idempotent by construction, and **refuses outright rather than counting a subset**. | *"VISIBLE UI RULE — ABSOLUTE. NO visible Milla, Vida or Website UI change may be implemented without Founder preview approval first."* · *"You may GROUP the remaining Vida questions in the return to save time. You may NOT answer them yourself."* · *"DO NOT create duplicate campaign/sequence objects if a current valid one already exists."* · *"Do not invent a cadence if none is locked."* · *"DO NOT invent a large versioning system."* · *"House must NEVER create fake: Stripe payments, invoices, customer revenue, programme revenue, ROI"* · *"Historical House evidence must be PRESERVED, not deleted."* | 7 Sep | `lib/preparation-readiness.ts` (pure core + IO shell) · `lib/programme.ts` (`markReadyForApproval` gated) · `supabase/migrations/20260907_programme_sourcing_authority.sql` · `routes/icps.ts` (house reserves, opens a batch, raises no PDL alarm) · `preparation-readiness.test.ts` (24, **RED** on an absent module; 5 teeth) · `house-programme-accounting.test.ts` (15; 7 teeth) · ⛓️ **AR5** · **AR8** · **R106** |

| **R108** | 🛑 **PREPARATION HAPPENS BEFORE APPROVAL, AND THE EXACT WORK APPROVED IS THE EXACT WORK ALLOWED TO RUN (7 Sep, founder-locked).** ⛓️ **THIS REVERSES A PRIOR SHAPE, DELIBERATELY** — `prepareProgrammeOutreach` was gated `PREPARABLE = ['APPROVED','LIVE']`, i.e. strictly AFTER approval, so the customer was asked to approve work that could not yet exist. 🛑 **① PREPARATION IS NOW STAGED.** *Pre-approval* requires **P1 and nothing weaker** (Payment 1 authorises sourcing and preparation only); *post-approval* keeps the old rule byte-for-byte — an approval recorded AND P2. ⚠️ **AND PREPARING GRANTS NOTHING**, which is proved structurally rather than promised: the pre-approval campaign is created as a **DRAFT**, because `activate: true` is the ONLY door to `status: 'active'` — the status the outreach machinery looks for — and it stays shut until Make Live, where `assertGoingLive` still demands an approval and Payment 2; a pre-approval **enrolment is inert** because `OUTREACH` authority requires approval AND P2 AND status LIVE. `autoEnrollLead` accepts a draft campaign **only** through verified `programmeFulfilment`; every ordinary caller still gets `active` only. 🛑 **② THE SEQUENCE HAS A POSITIVE LINK AT LAST.** `figsy_sequences` was CLIENT-scoped, so "this programme's sequence" could only be answered by picking one of the client's — and House carries sequences from a retired per-lead desk, so the words of an old campaign could be shown to a customer as the words they are approving. `figsy_sequences.campaign_id` (nullable, **no backfill** — a guessed campaign IS the leak) makes the chain **programme → `icps.programme_id` → `figsy_campaigns.icp_id` → `figsy_sequences.campaign_id`**, walked in ONE module so readiness and the frozen snapshot cannot be two different resolutions of "the sequence". **Ambiguity is a refusal, never a pick.** 🛑 **③ APPROVAL NOW RECORDS *WHAT*, NOT ONLY *WHEN*.** `approved_preparation_hash` is a sha256 over a canonical, timestamp-free digest of batch **+ its membership**, campaign, sequence, **ordered step content**, cadence, sender identity and the **enrolled set** — because an id is not the work: `batch_id` alone would not notice 200 different people, and `sequence_id` alone would not notice the words rewritten under it. Written in the **same conditional UPDATE** as `status = 'APPROVED'` (both doors), so nothing is ever stamped approved before an approval happens, and a programme that cannot be described **cannot be approved**. `checkProgrammeAuthority(..., 'OUTREACH')` — the one door — refuses `preparation_changed` when the current digest differs, and **an approved programme with NO stored hash is `unreadable`, not `unchanged`**: "we have no record of what was approved" must never resolve to "yes, it matches". ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE, STATED: every programme approved before this deploy must be RE-APPROVED before anything can send.** ⚠️ **AND NO CADENCE WAS INVENTED** — `cadenceIsConfigured` is the TEST for one (≥2 steps, every follow-up wait > 0), never a default, so it blocks honestly until the founder's approved cadence is persisted. | *"Campaign + sequence + messaging + cadence + sender + prepared enrolments must exist before the client is asked to approve."* · *"Preparation is NON-SENDING."* · *"P1 allows sourcing/preparation only. Approval does not send. P2 does not Make Live. Make Live does not broadly enable uncontrolled sending."* · *"Do not accept client-only sequence inference for current programme work."* · *"Do not silently relink historical rows to current programme work."* · *"the exact work approved must be the exact work allowed to run"* · *"Do not invent a cadence if none is locked."* · *"Do not create fake approval authority merely to make readiness pass."* · *"DO NOT invent a large versioning system."* | 7 Sep | `lib/programme-preparation.ts` (`preparationStageFor` · draft-before-approval) · `lib/programme-chain.ts` · `lib/preparation-snapshot.ts` · `lib/preparation-readiness.ts` · `lib/programme-authority.ts` (`preparation_changed`) · `lib/programme.ts` (both approval doors) · `supabase/migrations/20260907_preparation_snapshot.sql` · `preparation-before-approval.test.ts` (23) · `preparation-snapshot.test.ts` (27) · **25 teeth, all biting** · ⛓️ **R107** · **AR5** · **AR8** |

| **R109** | 🛑 **ONE CANONICAL SEQUENCE, A REVIEW-BOUNDARY FREEZE, AND OUTBOUND HAS A TIME OF DAY (8 Sep, founder-locked).** ⛓️ **① `figsy_sequences` IS CANONICAL FOR PROGRAMME WORK.** There were TWO stores: the customer reviewed the sequence resolved through `programme → ICP → campaign → figsy_sequences`, and `autoEnrollLead` built every enrolment from `figsy_campaigns.settings.sequence` — same client, same campaign, different words, nothing anywhere saying so. The programme path now reads the canonical store for review, readiness, the snapshot, the enrolment step count and send execution; **no fallback** to the settings copy, to `client_id`, to a newest row or to the AI draft path — a missing canonical sequence **refuses**, because an enrolment built from words nobody approved is worse than no enrolment, since it looks prepared. `figsy_enrollments.sequence_id` makes the copy checkable, and the send door refuses an enrolment pointed anywhere else. ⛓️ **② THE FREEZE MOVED TO THE REVIEW BOUNDARY.** Freezing only at APPROVED proved what was approved and **nothing about what was READ** — work could change underneath a client mid-review and the approval would faithfully record the NEW state, a perfect record of consent to something nobody looked at. `markReadyForApproval` now freezes `review_preparation_hash/_snapshot/_at` **in the same write as the status**; approval **recomputes only to COMPARE** and then stores the **REVIEWED** snapshot — never a fresh one. `READY_FOR_APPROVAL` left the preparable set entirely: preparation ADDS ENROLMENTS, so "idempotent recovery" there could change the very set being read. ⛓️ **③ THERE WAS NO SEND SCHEDULE AT ALL** — `getDay`, `getHours` and "send window" appear nowhere in the send path, so the product was ready to cold-email UK founders at 03:00 on a Sunday. `programmes.send_schedule` + `maySendNow` judge days and window **in the RECIPIENT's local time** (UK and US are five to eight hours apart; one UTC window puts half the audience in the middle of the night), **NULL refuses** for programme work, and the guard sits at the single OUTREACH door so cron, operator Run, retries, day-1, both provider pushes and LinkedIn all inherit it — **default-on**, with campaign ACTIVATION the one explicit opt-out because turning a campaign on is not a touch on a prospect. ⛓️ **④ FOUR ENROLMENT CRITERIA WERE ADDED AND ONE OF MY OWN CLAIMS WAS WRONG:** current batch (positive `batch_id`, so no older batch joins the reviewed set), canonical sequence, and a **verified business address re-proved at enrolment** (`isBusinessEmail` + `apollo_consented`, no new column) — while **bounce suppression already existed** through `opt_out_blocklist.reason` = `hard_bounce`/`spam_complaint`, which preparation already read. ⛓️ **⑤ `if (skipped >= 3)` WAS A REAL DEFECT** — on a 5-step sequence a `skip_next` at step 2 marked the enrolment COMPLETED and steps 4–5 silently never sent, to somebody who had just replied. Now read from the enrolment's own `steps`/`total_steps`; **not** replaced by a hardcoded 5. ⛓️ **⑥ SENDER SAFETY:** an unbroken TIE between equally-ranked mailboxes refuses (a frozen snapshot whose sender is a coin toss freezes nothing) and the same address live on another client refuses (two programmes as one human, one reputation, blind to each other's suppression) — **#610 rotation is untouched**, since a second box at a lower rank is still deterministic. ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE, STATED: every programme approved before this deploy must be RE-APPROVED, and no programme can send until a schedule is configured.** | *"figsy_sequences is the canonical sequence store for current programme work."* · *"Do not keep dual active truth for programme sending."* · *"These are launch requirements. Do not ask Founder whether to enforce them. Fix them."* · *"Do NOT invent a new duplicate bounce system if delivery/send history already has one."* · *"The client must review the exact thing they later approve."* · *"Do not silently approve a freshly changed current state."* · *"Once READY_FOR_APPROVAL is reached: material preparation is frozen."* · *"BUILD A SMALL SEND-DAY / SEND-WINDOW GUARD. Do not launch with round-the-clock outbound."* · *"Do not let historical code determine the product."* · *"duplicate physical sender across clients must fail closed"* | 8 Sep | `lib/send-schedule.ts` · `lib/programme-sender.ts` · `lib/programme-chain.ts` · `lib/preparation-snapshot.ts` (`reviewDrift`) · `lib/programme-authority.ts` (`outside_send_window` · `sender_unsafe` · `sequence_not_canonical`) · `lib/figsy.ts` (canonical steps · `sequenceTotalFor`) · `lib/programme-preparation.ts` · `supabase/migrations/20260908_review_freeze_and_schedule.sql` · 4 new suites, **25 teeth, all biting** · ⛓️ **R107** · **R108** |

| **R110** | 🛑 **RECIPIENT-LOCAL MEANS RECIPIENT-LOCAL, AND NO MODEL-WRITTEN EMAIL LEAVES WITHOUT THE SUPPRESSION FLOOR (8 Sep, founder-locked).** ⛓️ **① THE SCHEDULE FIX WAS WRONG IN THE SAME FAMILY AS THE BUG IT FIXED, and the founder caught it.** It mapped `United States → America/New_York` and called the result recipient-local. **08:30 New York is 05:30 Los Angeles** — a West Coast founder would have been cold-emailed at half past five in the morning by the guard built to prevent exactly that, with a log saying the send was inside an 08:30 window. **A guess dressed as a fact is worse than an admitted unknown.** **THE HIERARCHY:** ① a persisted IANA zone → ② a state/region that resolves deterministically → ③ a country whose zone SET is known, where **every** zone must allow (the intersection: the window opens at 08:30 in the westernmost zone and closes at 17:00 in the easternmost, so nobody in the set can be reached before their own 08:30) → ④ **FAIL CLOSED**. ⚠️ **`default_tz` NO LONGER GRANTS ANYTHING** — falling back to our home zone is the same defect as assuming New York. ⚠️ **DST IS NEVER HAND-ROLLED:** `Intl` applies the real rules for the real instant, so London is GMT in January and BST in July with no branch; `America/Phoenix` is its own zone precisely because Arizona does not observe DST. ⚠️ **AND THE DATA TRUTH IS STATED, NOT ASSUMED: `leads.country` is the ONLY geographic column** — no city, state, region or timezone is persisted anywhere, and `RevealedPerson` extracts only `email`, `email_status`, `country`, `last_name` though Apollo's reveal carries more. So tiers ① and ② are **threaded but empty today** and every American resolves through the intersection; persisting the reveal's state is the named follow-up that sharpens it. **House default: Mon–Fri, 08:30–17:00 recipient-local, mailbox `daily_cap`, cadence `[0,3,4,5,6]` — NOT persisted.** ⛓️ **② THE REPLY EXCEPTION WAS ASSERTED BEFORE IT WAS EARNED, and tracing it found an unclassified automated sender.** `sendManualReply` is genuinely **HUMAN-MANUAL**: two HTTP callers, both carrying a human-typed body, nothing else in the codebase calls it — and pause, terminal, an unreadable model, the kill-switch, demo mode, the blocklist and the mailbox all still bite; only money and approval are forgiven, because a prospect left mid-conversation is the worse failure. What a reply CAUSES is **AUTOMATED-EXECUTION** and fully governed (`checkEnrollmentAuthority('OUTREACH')` — approval, P2, LIVE, canonical sequence, sender safety, the approved-preparation comparison and the window). 🛑 **BUT `routes/founder.ts` HAD THREE MODEL-WRITTEN SENDERS WITH NO SAFETY GATE AT ALL** — the support agent classified an inbound email with Haiku and, if the model said `can_auto_reply`, generated a reply and sent it, so **somebody who told us to stop could email `hello@get-kind.com` and be answered by a model.** ⚠️ **PROGRAMME AUTHORITY WOULD BE THE WRONG BOUNDARY** — no programme, no campaign, no sequence, no prospect; approval and Payment 2 have nothing to say about K.I.N.D's own desk. **The correct minimal boundary is SUPPRESSION**, and it now runs through the existing shared `checkSendAllowed` gate, fails closed, and still forwards the email to a human when it refuses. | *"That is NOT recipient-local scheduling."* · *"We cannot send to a West Coast prospect at 05:30 while claiming the send window is 08:30 recipient-local."* · *"if safe recipient-local time cannot be established, FAIL CLOSED rather than silently pretending New York is local time"* · *"do not assume all US = Eastern"* · *"Do not build a giant timezone product. KISS."* · *"No AI/system-generated outbound message may inherit a 'human reply' exception."* · *"Do not automatically force replies into the exact original outbound sequence hash if that would be conceptually wrong. Instead determine the correct minimal authority boundary."* | 8 Sep | `lib/send-schedule.ts` (`resolveRecipientZones` · intersection · `REGION_ZONES`) · `lib/programme-authority.ts` (`OutreachContext` precision order) · `routes/founder.ts` (`agentMayEmail`) · `send-schedule.test.ts` (24) · `reply-authority.test.ts` (10) · **17 teeth, all biting** · ⛓️ **R109** |

| **R111** | 🎯 **THE APPROVED HOUSE SEQUENCE, HELD IN THE REPO — AND THE TWO THINGS THAT WOULD HAVE MADE IT UNREACHABLE (8 Sep, founder-approved).** The five messages, the cadence and the send schedule are founder-locked verbatim and live in `lib/house-sequence.ts` — **version-controlled, not typed into a database**, so "what did House send?" is answerable from the repo and a change is a diff somebody reviews. `applyHouseProgrammeSequence` writes them to the canonical `figsy_sequences` row and `programmes.send_schedule`; it is **operator-invoked after the migrations**, called by nothing, refuses rather than creating a campaign, is idempotent, and touches **two rows** — never `figsy_campaigns.settings.sequence`, which would recreate the dual truth. 🛑 **① `channel: 'email'` WAS MISSING AND WOULD HAVE SILENTLY SENT AI COPY.** `sequence-apply.ts` filters every stored sequence through `emailSteps`, which keeps only `channel === 'email'`. The steps `programme-chain.ts` resolved carried no channel, so they were filtered to **nothing** — `buildDraftFromSequence` returned null, `usingSequence` went false, and `autoEnrollLead` fell through to the **AI draft path**. The enrolment would have carried `sequence_id` pointing at the approved words while containing words a model invented, and **every downstream guard would have been satisfied by it.** The chain now preserves `channel` (absent defaults to `email`, because a step with a subject and a body IS an email step) and the House steps state it. 🛑 **② THE LOCKED CADENCE ARRAY AND THE LOCKED DAYS DISAGREED, and the literal array would have put two emails in one inbox on day one.** `steps[i].wait_days` is the wait **AFTER** step i (`send-due.ts` passes the current step's value as `waitDaysNext`), so `[0,3,4,5,6]` gives Day 0 · **0** · 3 · 7 · 12. The DAYS are the product decision and are stated unambiguously, so they are honoured and the array is re-expressed as the waits that produce them: **`[3,4,5,6,0]` → Day 0 · 3 · 7 · 12 · 18.** ⚠️ **FLAGGED, NOT QUIETLY CHOSEN** — if the array was meant literally, the intended schedule is Day 0 · 0 · 3 · 7 · 12 and the file is wrong. ⛓️ **AND `cadenceIsConfigured` WAS CHECKING THE WRONG END** for the same reason — it ignored the FIRST value (the "wait before" reading) when the value never read is the LAST. Corrected: every gap BETWEEN real steps must be a real gap. ⚠️ **US STAYS ALL-ZONE** (founder-locked): the House ICP says US, and until state/timezone is persisted we must not silently assume Eastern or silently narrow the ICP. | *"Persist the five steps and cadence into the positively linked House programme sequence."* · *"Do NOT persist programme sequence truth into figsy_campaigns.settings.sequence except where legacy/non-programme compatibility genuinely requires that old field."* · *"Do not hardcode sequence length globally."* · *"Keep the safe all-US-zone intersection behaviour. Do not narrow US to lower 48."* · *"Do not hardcode 50/day."* · *"Do NOT move production programme state."* | 8 Sep | `lib/house-sequence.ts` (the five messages · `HOUSE_CADENCE` · `HOUSE_SEND_SCHEDULE` · `applyHouseProgrammeSequence`) · `lib/programme-chain.ts` (`channel` preserved) · `lib/preparation-readiness.ts` (`cadenceIsConfigured` corrected) · `house-sequence.test.ts` (17) · **12 teeth, all biting** · ⛓️ **R109** · **R110** |

| **R112** | 🎯 **THE CADENCE IS `[3,4,5,6,0]`, AND THE ORCHESTRATOR OWNS THE ORDER (8 Sep, founder-locked — final).** ⛓️ **① TWO NOTATIONS, ONE PERSISTED FORM.** *Absolute campaign day* (Day 0 · 3 · 7 · 12 · 18 — the product decision) and *wait after the current step* (what `steps[i].wait_days` means in this codebase: `send-due.ts` passes the CURRENT step's value as `waitDaysNext`, and `sendSequenceEmailCore` schedules the next send that many days out). **The persisted form is `[3,4,5,6,0]`; `[0,3,4,5,6]` must never be persisted** — it waits ZERO days after step 1 and puts two emails in one prospect's inbox on day one. The terminal `0` is unread. The distinction is now written into the code, the tests and this row, because it has already been confused once and the failure is silent. Sequence length stays configurable; nothing is hardcoded to five. ⛓️ **② THE HELPER WAS AN ORPHAN, AND THAT WAS UNSAFE.** `applyHouseProgrammeSequence` was called by nothing, so the only route through production was: run preparation (creates the campaign, refuses to enrol), remember to apply the sequence, run preparation again. **A production path that depends on somebody remembering a magic call order is a path that will one day be run in the wrong order** — and the wrong order here means enrolments built from words nobody approved. **`prepareProgrammeOutreach` now owns the order:** ICP → draft campaign → **canonical sequence (seeded for House)** → **cadence validated** → enrolments → freeze → READY_FOR_APPROVAL. Exactly ONE caller; a second would be a second preparation system. ⛓️ **③ GATED TO HOUSE BY PROVED IDENTITY, NEVER INFERRED.** `audienceForClientStrict` answers from the AUTH USER and **THROWS** rather than guessing; a throw is read as *not house*. The approved copy is Client Zero's — applying it to a paying customer's programme would put M&V's own pitch in front of THEIR prospects. Not `HOUSE_CLIENT_ID`, not a name, not a `client_id` guess. ⚠️ **IT ONLY SEEDS:** it runs solely when the chain resolved NO sequence, so an edited House sequence is never reset. ⚠️ **AND EVERY OTHER PROGRAMME STILL REFUSES** — a customer programme with no sequence is told to author one, never given ours and never allowed to AI-draft. | *"The correct internal persisted representation is `[3,4,5,6,0]`. LOCK THIS. Do NOT persist `[0,3,4,5,6]`."* · *"Make the semantics explicit … absolute campaign day vs wait after current step."* · *"Do not require a human/operator to remember a magic manual call order."* · *"`applyHouseProgrammeSequence` should not remain an orphan helper that production depends on someone manually invoking later."* · *"Do not silently apply House copy to every customer."* · *"Do not infer House merely from `client_id` if that can hit other programmes."* | 8 Sep | `lib/house-sequence.ts` (`HOUSE_CADENCE = [3,4,5,6,0]`) · `lib/programme-preparation.ts` (the ordered orchestrator · House gate · cadence validation) · `house-sequence.test.ts` (28, incl. the executable day arithmetic) · **14 teeth, all biting** · ⛓️ **R111** |
| **R113** | 🎯 **THE APPROVED FIVE-STEP SEQUENCE IS THE LAUNCH COPY FOR *ONE* PROGRAMME, NAMED EXPLICITLY (8 Sep, founder-locked).** 🛑 **THE DEFECT THIS CLOSES.** The auto-seed gate was `audienceForClientStrict(client_id) === 'house'`. That proves a **CLASSIFICATION, not an identity**: every House programme satisfies it — a second one created next month, a different ICP under the same client, every historical one, every future one. The genuine gap was K.I.N.D's own sending identities, which hold **no `client_inboxes` row at all** and so passed that filter. **③ HOUSE IDENTITY.** There are two House-shaped environment facts and only one is an identity: `HOUSE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL` → `resolveHouseUserIds` → `decideHouseClient` is canonical; **`HOUSE_CLIENT_ID` is the PARKED Instantly/Smartlead push switch, documented "Do NOT set"**, and no safety or money path may read it — setting it would hand one client House's exemptions. Pinned as an **inventory** so a third reader cannot appear unnoticed. 🛑 **WHAT EARNED THE REPLY HALF.** `routeReply(matches, null)` returned **every** match across **every** client and the pipeline wrote a reply row for each. That was correct under R1 while one shared Resend inbox was the only inbound path — and **R129's automatic sender assignment is what made it live**, because any provider that does not report `to` still lands there. | **R132** | 📥 **AN AMBIGUOUS REPLY IS RETAINED, VISIBLE AND HUMAN-DECIDED — A PRIVACY FIX MAY NOT BE PAID FOR IN LOST MAIL (17 Sep, founder-locked).** His words, verbatim: *"We must not replace a privacy leak with silent data loss."* The required chain: *"ambiguous reply → durable retention → visible Vida exception → human chooses correct client OR intentionally discards → chosen reply enters normal reply processing once → auditable + idempotent."* 🛑 **WHAT EARNED IT.** R131 correctly stopped one external reply reaching two clients — and the refused reply then existed **only in process memory**: Resend's `email.received` webhook is metadata-only, so the body had been fetched into a local variable; `processed_webhook_events` stores an id and a source and nothing else; the dedup claim was already taken; and the route answered **200**, which is a promise to the provider that we have the event. It never redelivers after a 200. The only notification was `sendFounderAlert`, and **`founder_alerts` has no reader anywhere in the product** — so the safety fix had no recovery path at all. ⚠️ **THE ORDER IS THE RULE, NOT AN IMPLEMENTATION DETAIL.** Verbatim: *"Do NOT mark the unattributed reply resolved BEFORE normal reply processing succeeds."* · *"If reply processing fails: the exception must remain recoverable."* · *"Do not create a state where: `resolved_at` is set but no client-visible reply exists."* · *"A repeated operator action must not create duplicate `figsy_replies`."* So: **validate candidate → CLAIM → write the reply → record the resolution**, with the claim a separate compare-and-set column that a failed attribution hands back. One column cannot satisfy both halves. ⚠️ **AND THE CHOSEN CLIENT MUST HAVE BEEN A STORED CANDIDATE** — the candidate set is persisted **as it was at ingest**, because leads move and a resolution weeks later must be checked against the clients who actually held that prospect when the reply arrived. Verbatim: *"Resolution cannot silently change to a different client later."* ⚠️ **NO NEW PLATFORM, AND `founder_alerts` IS NOT THE AUTHORITY.** Verbatim: *"Do NOT build another alert platform."* · *"Do not build a reader for `founder_alerts` in this ticket."* The exception enters Vida's **existing** `GET /operator/alerts` feed; the alert email stays as secondary notification only. ⚠️ **NOR A SECOND REPLY PIPELINE:** *"Do NOT create a second reply-processing implementation."* The resolved reply is written by `processInboundReply` with one extra field naming the owner — which routes it through the existing `how: 'inbox'` branch, so *"It must NOT re-enable fan-out"* holds by construction rather than by care. ⚠️ **TWO SMALLER LOCKS, BOTH HIS:** *"Do NOT make `provider_event_key` globally unique by itself"* (event ids are provider-scoped — a global unique lets one provider's reply suppress another's, the exact trap `replyEventKey` namespaces against), and *"Do not label the destructive action merely 'Not ours'"* — with several candidates that is ambiguous, so the button reads **Discard reply** and means *belongs to none of them*. **The reply body is deliberately absent from the alert label**, per *"Do not expose reply body in a generic list."* | *"We must not replace a privacy leak with silent data loss."* · *"Do NOT mark the unattributed reply resolved BEFORE normal reply processing succeeds."* · *"If reply processing fails: the exception must remain recoverable."* · *"A repeated operator action must not create duplicate figsy_replies."* · *"Do NOT create a second reply-processing implementation."* · *"It must NOT re-enable fan-out."* · *"Do NOT build another alert platform."* · *"Do NOT make provider_event_key globally unique by itself."* · *"Do not label the destructive action merely 'Not ours'."* | 17 Sep | ⛓️ **23 Sep (checked against the code): MERGED AND LIVE — every commit citing this rule is in the live build `6a93c77`; | **R132a** | 🔁 **⛓️ AMENDS R132 THE SAME DAY — A WRITTEN REPLY WITH FAILED BOOKKEEPING MUST BE RECONCILABLE, NOT STUCK (17 Sep, founder-locked).** R132's order is unchanged and still correct: **validate candidate → claim → write the reply → record the resolution.** 🛑 **WHAT IT LEFT BEHIND.** When the final settlement failed, the reply existed, the exception stayed CLAIMED, and a retry was refused with a 409 to avoid a duplicate reply. Safe, and **with no exit except editing the database by hand** — which is not an exit. His words: *"Do not require the operator or Founder to manually edit the database."* ⚠️ **THE ANSWER WAS ALREADY PERSISTED, AND NO NEW ARCHITECTURE WAS ADDED.** `figsy_replies.provider_event_key` carries `unattributed:<retention id>`, written by R132 for exactly this purpose and protected by the pre-existing partial unique index. Verbatim: *"USE THAT EXISTING CORRELATION."* · *"Do NOT invent another attribution architecture."* **ZERO new migrations.** 🛑 **AND IT READS NOTHING ELSE.** Verbatim: *"Do not use: sender email alone / prospect email alone / timestamps / newest record / candidate order / fuzzy matching. The persisted correlation is authority."* **The four cases:** ① no existing reply → the R132 path, byte-unchanged; ② exactly one → **reconcile only** — settle the exception, do **not** reprocess, reclassify, or re-fire CRM/hot-path; ③ lookup unreadable → **FAIL CLOSED** (no reprocess, no settle, no discard); ④ more than one client → **FAIL CLOSED, pick neither.** ⚠️ **THE PERSISTED REPLY WINS.** A retry naming a different candidate is **refused** — verbatim *"Resolution cannot silently change to a different client later"* — because the alternative is either a second reply or a record the data contradicts. ⚠️ **AND RECONCILIATION RUNS BEFORE THE CLAIM**, which is what lets it recover a claim a previous attempt left held; it writes nothing, so it needs no claim, and `settleUnattributedReply`'s own compare-and-set is the concurrency guard. No change to the claim code was required. 🛑 **DISCARD IS THE SAME DEFECT'S OTHER HALF.** A reply already written to a client could have been recorded `resolution = 'discarded'` — a durable lie about a client-visible record, while that client reads and possibly answers the message. Discard now **refuses with a 409** naming the client and the remedy. Of the two the founder allowed, refusal was chosen over silent auto-attribution: **Discard means "belongs to none of them", and converting that click into an attribution would do something the operator did not ask for, to a client they did not name, on a surface with no undo.** | *"Do not require the operator or Founder to manually edit the database."* · *"USE THAT EXISTING CORRELATION."* · *"Do NOT invent another attribution architecture."* · *"The persisted correlation is authority."* · *"under no circumstance may the system say: resolution='discarded' when the reply has already been written to a client"* · *"Resolution cannot silently change to a different client later."* | 17 Sep | ⛓️ **23 Sep (checked against the code): MERGED AND LIVE — every commit citing this rule is in the live build `6a93c77`; migrations applied (founder, Vida → Engine, 23 Sep).** **BUILT ON BRANCH, UNMERGED, UNDEPLOYED.** `existingAttributionFor` (one indexed read of `provider_event_key`; fails closed on unreadable **and** on spanning clients) · both operator routes · **32 new assertions · 6 teeth proved RED then restored** · **NO NEW MIGRATION** — the two from R129/R132 remain unapplied. ➕ **AND THE TEETH FOUND A LEAK IN MY OWN HARNESS:** the recovery suite reassigned its store in `beforeEach`, so a `vi.doMock` closure could keep writing into a previous test's object — the route reported success while the row under assertion stayed pristine, and the test passed alone and failed in the full file. Both restored.** ➕ **PR C (23 Sep) — ④'S WALLET CREDIT, `check.sh` GREEN (484 files · 10,357 tests) AND RUNTIME VERIFIED against a real PostgreSQL (108 real-DB tests, disposable harness — NOT production).** `settleProgrammeShortfall` computes the credit from what was actually COLLECTED, claims the settlement with a compare-and-set on the new `shortfall_credited_at`, pays via the atomic `increment_wallet` RPC, writes a `wallet_topup` ledger row keyed `programme-shortfall:<id>`, and adds the credit to `make_whole_cents` — **which is what stops a partner earning 25% of money we gave back** (R78/R68). ⚠️ **`increment_wallet` IS NOT IDEMPOTENT**, so the claim is the only thing between a double-clicked operator button and a wallet credited twice; a failed wallet move RELEASES the claim so a retry needs no hand-edited database (R132a), while a failed LEDGER row after the money moved does **not** release — it alerts, because releasing would invite a second credit. ⚠️ **DELIVERED MEETINGS ARE PERSISTED, NEVER RE-DERIVED** — meetings keep being booked elsewhere and a settled figure may not move. **Red-proved: removing the compare-and-set fails the concurrency guard; removing both idempotency layers fails 4, including the silent double-reduction of `make_whole_cents`.** 🛑 **AND THE MIGRATION'S FIRST CONSTRAINT WAS WRONG AND WAS CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED:** `CHECK ((credited_at IS NULL) = (cents = 0))` reads as obviously correct and would have **refused the settlement of every SUCCESSFUL programme** — one that delivered everything it sold is still settled, it simply owes nothing. No unit test could have caught it (a mock has no constraints); the real-DB suite now asserts both directions. ➕ **ONE AUTHORISED EXCEPTION RECORDED, NOT RENAMED AROUND:** `milla-billing-usage.test.ts` forbids a Milla-era migration whose key matches `/credit|wallet|billing/`, and it fired on this one CORRECTLY. Renaming the migration would have defeated the guard by wordplay, so the key is listed as authorised and every other money-model migration in that window still fails. ➕ **AND THE SPEND PATH LANDED IN THE SAME PR (23 Sep), ON THREE FOUNDER ANSWERS.** **Q1 — which payment may a credit reduce? *P1.*** P1 authorises sourcing, so the credit reduces the cost of STARTING the next run, which is what *"towards another icp run"* means; P2 is going live and takes none. Enforced in `createProgrammeCheckoutSession` itself, not only at the caller. **Q2 — may it cover a payment in full? *No.*** `walletCreditForPayment` leaves at least `MIN_CASH_PAYMENT_CENTS` going through Stripe, and a CHECK says the same thing so a caller that forgets the cap cannot write the state. A full cover would mean no Stripe session at all — Stripe rejects a zero checkout — and a wallet-only payment fits neither side of the existing P1 authority XOR. **Q3 — does commission follow cash rather than price? *yes.*** 🛑 **AND THIS ONE CLOSES A SILENT DOUBLE COUNT.** `computeContribution` read revenue off `first_payment_cents` — what was OWED — which until now was also what ARRIVED. A P1 part-paid from the wallet takes less cash, and those cents were already revenue on the programme that credited them back: counted twice, contribution overstated, and a partner paid 25% of money that never came in. R68's shape exactly. `wallet_applied_cents` is now subtracted from revenue. ⚠️ **THE DRAW IS ON THE CONFIRMED PAYMENT, NEVER AT CHECKOUT CREATION** — an abandoned checkout must not spend a client's credit — and a LOST claim hands the credit back, because one payment must never spend it twice. It never draws more than the live balance and alerts when it draws less than was discounted. ➕ **NO FROZEN-FILE EDIT WAS NEEDED.** `house-authority.test.ts` pins the exact expression `(p.first_paid_at ? p.first_payment_cents : 0)` to prove contribution never reads internal authority — an intent untouched here — so the credit is subtracted as its own term rather than folded into that branch: identical arithmetic, guard intact. ➕ **AND TWO GUARDS GOT SHARPER RATHER THAN LOOSER:** `milla-programme.test.ts` banned the substring `wallet_balance` anywhere in the route and now asserts what it always meant — exactly ONE read, in the credit computation, never in a client payload; `p1-p2-money-boundary.test.ts`'s exhaustive P1 patch list gains the column and asserts it is zero when no credit applies. **Red-proved: reading revenue off price instead of cash fails 2; removing the lost-claim reversal fails the double-spend guard.** **`check.sh` GREEN — 485 files · 10,375 tests · 112 real-DB.** ⛓️ **23 Sep: both migrations APPLIED — the founder confirmed all migrations applied in Vida → Engine.** ~~⚠️ **WHAT IS STILL NOT BUILT:** both migrations are **UNAPPLIED**.~~ Until they run, `settleProgrammeShortfall` refuses by name and no credit is discounted — the safe direction. ⛓️ chains **R74** · **R77/FD-01** · **R81** · **R124** |

A reply alone is not one. **NOT GUARANTEED:** attendance · purchase · approved budget · progression · the value of any opportunity · the client's salesperson · any conversion or revenue outcome. **SHORTFALL → CREDIT:** each qualified meeting not delivered is credited at the per-meeting rate paid, against the **first 50% of the client's next programme**; any left over carries to the one after; never cash; does not expire; forfeited on termination for breach. **NO-SHOWS:** prospect no-show or cancellation → one reschedule at no charge · client cancels or does not attend → counts as delivered · a reschedule counts once · unrecoverable → a replacement at M&V's discretion, never cash · a properly qualified meeting is not credited back because the prospect later fails to attend. **CHALLENGES:** within **3 business days of the meeting being booked**, naming the condition not met; judged on what was known at booking, never on the sales outcome; not unqualified afterwards because the prospect does not progress, changes their mind, the call goes badly, there is no immediate budget, the opportunity is smaller or they do not buy. **RESPONSIBILITY:** M&V — the right person → genuine interest → qualified against agreed criteria → the meeting secured · the client — attending → the sales conversation → discovery → proposal → negotiation → closing. *"M&V should be paid for the outcome it controls."* **THE OUTCOME IS NAMED, NEVER LEFT OPEN (same day, second ruling).** Verbatim: *"you need to give what the outcome is. i made mention on the paste i gave you earlier"* — the paste: *"the definition of the outcome is too open to dispute."* So every "outcome" that meant the thing bought became **"qualified meetings"** or **"meeting target"**;
